1: # AGENTS.md
2: 
3: Rules for any AI coding agent working in this repository. Read this fully before making changes. Also read `PRD_Webhook_Delivery_Service_fix.pdf` (the source of truth for scope and design) and `DECISIONS.md`.
4: 
5: ## 1. Project context
6: 
7: - **What:** An internal Go service that accepts events via `POST /events` and reliably delivers them over HTTP POST to customer-registered endpoints, with retries, status querying, and customer isolation.
8: - **Why it exists:** This is a time-boxed take-home test (~3 hours). The goal is a **small, well-reasoned solution with clear trade-offs**, not an exhaustive one. The owner must be able to explain and defend every decision in an interview.
9: - **Owner:** The human developer. They direct; you implement. Never make a design decision silently.
10: 
11: ## 2. Hard rules (never violate)
12: 
13: 1. **Git is off-limits.** Do NOT run `git commit`, `git push`, `git add`, `git tag`, `git merge`, `git rebase`, `git reset`, `git checkout`, `git stash`, or any command that changes repository history, branches, or remotes. The owner handles all version control. Read-only commands (`git status`, `git diff`, `git log`) are allowed.
14: 2. **No GitHub/remote interaction.** No `gh` CLI, no creating PRs/issues, no pushing, no changing remotes.
15: 3. **Stay inside scope.** Do not build anything listed under non-goals (section 3). If you think something out of scope is needed, stop and ask.
16: 4. **No destructive actions without asking.** Do not delete files or directories, drop databases, or run `rm -rf`. Ask first.
17: 5. **No secrets or network calls to real services.** Customer endpoints are always mocked. Do not add telemetry or call external APIs.
18: 6. **No new dependencies without approval.** Prefer the Go standard library. If a dependency is truly needed, explain why and wait for confirmation. Pre-approved: a pure-Go SQLite driver (e.g. `modernc.org/sqlite`).
19: 7. **Do not silently change the PRD's design decisions.** If code and PRD conflict, flag it and ask which wins.
20: 
21: ## 3. Scope
22: 
23: ### In scope
24: - HTTP API: `POST /events`, `GET /events/{id}`, `GET /events` (stretch), subscription registration (endpoint or seed config), `/healthz`.
25: - SQLite datastore (source of truth), transactional claims with leases.
26: - Dispatcher + fixed worker pool delivering via HTTP POST with per-request timeout.
27: - Retry with exponential backoff + jitter, max delay, and overall deadline; terminal `failed` state.
28: - Per-customer concurrency cap and per-endpoint circuit breaker.
29: - Graceful shutdown, structured logs, simple counters.
30: - Mock customer endpoint tool and tests.
31: - `DECISIONS.md`.
32: 
33: ### Out of scope (do NOT build)
34: - Authentication / login, multi-tenant billing, production deployment (Docker/K8s/CI/CD).
35: - **Any UI.** No React frontend. A status API is enough.
36: - Multi-instance / distributed operation (single process only).
37: - Per-customer queues, strict ordering mode, replay UI. These are "next steps" to mention in `DECISIONS.md`, not to implement.
38: 
39: ## 4. Design decisions (locked, per PRD)
40: 
41: | Topic | Decision |
42: |---|---|
43: | Delivery semantics | **At-least-once.** Persist before returning 202. Duplicates possible; `event_id` is the idempotency key. |
44: | Retry | Exponential backoff, multiplier 2x, initial 10s, jitter ±20%, max delay 30 min, deadline 24h, per-request timeout 10s. All configurable. |
45: | Retry classification | Retry on network error, timeout, 5xx, 408, 429 (respect `Retry-After`). Other 4xx → fail early with reason recorded. |
46: | Terminal state | `failed` after deadline (or permanent 4xx). Never deleted; full attempt history kept. |
47: | Long outages | Shared worker pool (default 8) with bounded concurrency. Failed event = row with future `next_attempt_at`; workers are never parked on a dead endpoint. Per-customer cap (default 2 in-flight). Circuit breaker after 5 consecutive failures with growing cool-down. |
48: | Ordering | No strict guarantee. Oldest-due-first; deliveries carry `event_id`, `created_at`, and per-customer `sequence`. |
49: | Crash safety | Claim sets `lease_until`. Expired lease → event is picked up again. |
50: | Datastore | SQLite (pure Go), single process, one-line run. |
51: 
52: ## 5. Tech stack and layout
53: 
54: - **Language:** Go (latest stable the owner uses; check `go.mod`).
55: - **Run command (must keep working):** `go run ./cmd/server`
56: - **Suggested layout** (confirm before deviating):
57: 
58: ```
59: cmd/server/          # main entrypoint
60: cmd/mockendpoint/    # mock customer endpoint tool
61: internal/api/        # HTTP handlers, validation
62: internal/store/      # SQLite, migrations, queries
63: internal/dispatcher/ # scheduler, worker pool, claims, leases
64: internal/delivery/   # HTTP sender, attempt recording
65: internal/retry/      # backoff calculation, classification
66: internal/breaker/    # circuit breaker
67: internal/config/     # config with defaults
68: DECISIONS.md
69: AGENTS.md
70: ```
71: 
72: ## 6. Coding standards
73: 
74: - Idiomatic Go. Run `gofmt` and `go vet` before reporting work as done.
75: - Keep it **small and readable**. Prefer simple code over clever abstractions. No premature interfaces, no unused layers.
76: - Pass `context.Context` as the first parameter for anything that does I/O or can block. Respect cancellation.
77: - Wrap errors with context (`fmt.Errorf("claim event: %w", err)`). Never swallow errors.
78: - No global mutable state. Inject dependencies (store, clock, config).
79: - **Inject a clock** (`func() time.Time` or small interface) so backoff, leases, and deadlines are testable without sleeping.
80: - No goroutine leaks: every goroutine must have a clear stop condition tied to context/shutdown.
81: - All time-based parameters come from config with the PRD defaults, so tests can scale them down.
82: - Use parameterized SQL only. Never build queries with string concatenation of user input.
83: - Comments explain **why**, not what. Add a short comment wherever a trade-off or non-obvious decision lives (leases, claim query, breaker, jitter).
84: 
85: ## 7. Correctness rules specific to this system
86: 
87: - An event must be **persisted before** the 202 response is sent.
88: - Claiming a due event must be **atomic** (single transaction) so two workers never take the same row, and it must set `lease_until`.
89: - A worker must never block waiting for a dead endpoint between attempts; schedule via `next_attempt_at`.
90: - Always record a `delivery_attempts` row for every attempt (status, error, duration).
91: - Payload size limit and request body validation happen **before** storing; invalid input stores nothing (FR2).
92: - Never delete events. Terminal `failed` rows are retained.
93: - Graceful shutdown: stop claiming, let in-flight requests finish within a timeout, then exit.
94: - Outgoing request must include headers `X-Event-Id`, `X-Delivery-Attempt`, `Content-Type: application/json`, and body `{event_id, created_at, sequence, payload}`.
95: 
96: ## 8. Testing rules
97: 
98: - Write tests alongside the code they cover; do not leave testing to the end.
99: - **Required unit tests:** backoff calculation (bounds, jitter range, cap) and retry classification (which statuses retry).
100: - **Required scenarios** (via the mock endpoint, with times scaled down by config): happy path; endpoint down then recovers; long outage with isolation (other customers stay timely, breaker opens, stuck events end `failed` after deadline); slow endpoint (workers released at timeout, per-customer cap holds); crash mid-delivery (lease expires, retried, duplicate acceptable).
101: - Tests must be **deterministic and fast**. No real sleeps for long durations; use the injected clock or scaled config.
102: - Do not chase coverage percentage. Test the behaviors above.
103: - Run `go test ./...` (and `-race` where practical) before saying a task is done.
104: 
105: ## 9. Workflow with the owner
106: 
107: - **Work in small steps** following the PRD plan: (1) setup/schema/subscriptions, (2) ingest + status API, (3) dispatcher/workers/retry, (4) per-customer cap + breaker, (5) mock endpoint + tests, (6) `DECISIONS.md` + wrap-up. Finish and report one step before starting the next.
108: - **Explain as you go.** The owner must understand and defend the code in an interview. After each step, give a short summary: what changed, why, and what trade-off it involves.
109: - **Ask before deciding.** If a requirement is ambiguous or a choice has real trade-offs, present 2-3 options briefly with your recommendation instead of picking silently.
110: - **Be honest about limits.** If something is incomplete, untested, or you are unsure, say so. Never claim tests pass without running them.
111: - **Respect the time cap (~3 hours).** Prefer the smaller solution. If something is running long, stop and propose what to cut and note it as a "next step."
112: - **Do not over-engineer.** If you catch yourself adding abstractions, configuration, or features the PRD does not require, stop and remove them.
113: 
114: ## 10. AI transcript and deliverables
115: 
116: - The test requires the **complete, unedited transcript** of AI conversations to be submitted. Do not suggest editing, summarizing, or removing parts of it.
117: - Deliverables: code with a one-line run command, a short `DECISIONS.md` (half a page, covering delivery semantics, retry, long outages, ordering, and what was deliberately left out), the AI transcript, and an optional workflow reflection.
118: - When asked to draft `DECISIONS.md`, keep it concise and make sure it reflects what was **actually implemented**, not aspirations.
119: 
120: ## 11. Definition of done (per task)
121: 
122: - [ ] Code compiles; `gofmt` and `go vet` are clean.
123: - [ ] Relevant tests written and passing (`go test ./...`).
124: - [ ] Matches the PRD and locked decisions, or deviations were flagged to the owner.
125: - [ ] No out-of-scope features added; no new dependencies without approval.
126: - [ ] No git commands that modify state were run.
127: - [ ] Short summary given to the owner: what, why, trade-offs, what is left.

1: # PRD: Webhook Delivery Service (Go)
2: 
3: ## 1. Overview
4: 
5: An internal service that accepts events from producers and reliably delivers them over HTTP POST to endpoints that customers have registered. Customer endpoints can be slow, erroring or down for hours, and the service must not lose their events or let one bad endpoint hurt other customers.
6: 
7: The brief is deliberately underspecified. The goal of this project is a small, well-reasoned solution with clear trade-offs, not an exhaustive one. Every decision below must be defensible in the interview.
8: 
9: ## 2. Goals and non-goals
10: 
11: ### Goals
12: 
13: - Never lose an accepted event, including across a process crash or restart.
14: - Deliver each event at least once to the customer's registered endpoint.
15: - Retry failed deliveries with backoff until a deadline, then end in a clear terminal state.
16: - Let anyone query the delivery status of an event.
17: - Isolate customers from each other during outages.
18: 
19: ### Non-goals (out of scope per the brief)
20: 
21: - Authentication or login, multi-tenant billing, production deployment.
22: - A UI. The brief says no UI; a status API is enough, so no React frontend is built.
23: - Multi-instance or distributed operation (single process only; noted as a next step).
24: - Real customer endpoints. Endpoints are mocked.
25: 
26: ## 3. Functional requirements
27: 
28: | ID | Requirement |
29: |----|-------------|
30: | FR1 | `POST /events` accepts a customer/subscription identifier and a JSON payload, persists the event, and returns `202 Accepted` with an `event_id` without waiting for delivery. |
31: | FR2 | Invalid input (unknown subscription, malformed JSON, oversized payload) is rejected with a 4xx and nothing is stored. |
32: | FR3 | A background delivery loop sends each event to the subscription's endpoint via HTTP POST with a per-request timeout. |
33: | FR4 | A 2xx response marks the event delivered. Any other response, network error or timeout schedules a retry. |
34: | FR5 | Retries use exponential backoff with jitter, a maximum delay and an overall retry deadline. |
35: | FR6 | When the deadline passes, the event moves to terminal `failed`. It is kept, never deleted. |
36: | FR7 | `GET /events/{id}` returns status (`pending`, `delivered`, `failed`), attempt count, next attempt time and last error. |
37: | FR8 | `GET /events?subscription_id=&status=` lists events for inspection (stretch if time is short). |
38: | FR9 | Pending events survive a restart and resume delivery. |
39: | FR10 | Subscriptions (customer id plus endpoint URL) can be registered through a simple endpoint or seed config. No auth. |
40: 
41: ## 4. Key design decisions
42: 
43: ### 4.1 Delivery semantics: at-least-once
44: 
45: - The event is persisted before the producer gets a response. Delivery is asynchronous.
46: - A timeout or lost response cannot be distinguished from a failure, so it is retried. This means duplicates are possible.
47: - **Cost:** duplicates. **Demand on the customer:** idempotent handlers, using `event_id` as the idempotency key.
48: - **Why not at-most-once:** it can silently lose events, which contradicts the core requirement.
49: - **Why not exactly-once:** it is not achievable over HTTP without cooperation from the receiver.
50: 
51: ### 4.2 Retry behavior
52: 
53: Proposed defaults, all configurable:
54: 
55: | Parameter | Default |
56: |-----------|---------|
57: | Per-request timeout | 10s |
58: | Initial delay | 10s |
59: | Multiplier | 2x |
60: | Jitter | randomized (for example +/-20%) to avoid a thundering herd on recovery |
61: | Maximum delay | 30 min |
62: | Retry deadline | 24h from event creation |
63: 
64: - Retry on: network errors, timeouts, 5xx, 408, 429 (respect `Retry-After` if present).
65: - Do not retry endlessly on permanent client errors such as 4xx other than 408/429. Mark failed early, and record the reason.
66: - Terminal state: `failed` after the deadline, retained with its full attempt history so it can be replayed manually.
67: 
68: ### 4.3 Long outages: shared worker pool with bounded concurrency
69: 
70: Chosen over per-customer processing for simplicity and bounded resource use.
71: 
72: - A fixed pool of N workers (default 8) claims due deliveries from the database, oldest first.
73: - A failed event is just a row with a future `next_attempt_at`. A worker is never parked waiting on a dead endpoint, so a six-hour outage consumes no workers between attempts.
74: - Per-customer concurrency cap (default 2 in-flight) so a slow or backlogged customer cannot occupy the whole pool.
75: - Per-endpoint circuit breaker: after K consecutive failures (default 5), pause that endpoint for a cool-down that grows over time, instead of burning retries on every event.
76: - Short request timeout so slow endpoints release workers quickly.
77: - On recovery, the backlog drains in parallel within the per-customer cap.
78: 
79: **Trade-offs:** less isolation than dedicated per-customer queues, and no ordering from the pool itself. In exchange we avoid per-customer goroutine lifecycle management and keep the design small. Per-customer queues are listed as a next step.
80: 
81: ### 4.4 Ordering: no strict guarantee
82: 
83: - Workers pick the oldest due event first, so delivery is roughly chronological in the normal case. Retries and concurrent workers can still reorder events.
84: - Each delivery carries `event_id`, `created_at` and a per-customer `sequence` so consumers can dedupe and reorder.
85: - **Why not strict ordering:** it requires per-customer serialization, which lets one failing event block all later events for that customer during an outage.
86: - **Cost:** customers must tolerate out-of-order and duplicate deliveries.
87: - **Next step:** an opt-in ordered mode per subscription, with a limit on how long a blocked event can hold the queue.
88: 
89: ## 5. Architecture
90: 
91: Single Go process with three parts:
92: 
93: 1. **HTTP API:** ingest, status and subscription endpoints.
94: 2. **Datastore:** source of truth for events, subscriptions and attempts. Proposed: SQLite (pure-Go driver) for a zero-setup one-line run, with transactional claims. Postgres is the production-like alternative using `FOR UPDATE SKIP LOCKED`; the cost is needing Docker to run.
95: 3. **Dispatcher and workers:** a scheduler polls for due events and hands them to the worker pool, which performs the HTTP calls and records each attempt.
96: 
97: **Flow:** producer posts event, API validates and stores it as `pending` with `next_attempt_at = now`, API returns 202. A worker claims the row with a lease, POSTs to the endpoint, then marks delivered, or schedules the next attempt, or marks failed past the deadline.
98: 
99: **Crash safety:** a claim sets a lease expiry. If the process dies mid-delivery, the lease expires and the event is picked up again. This is consistent with at-least-once.
100: 
101: **Graceful shutdown:** stop claiming, let in-flight requests finish within a timeout, then exit.
102: 
103: ## 6. Data model
104: 
105: ```
106: subscriptions:     id, customer_id, endpoint_url, created_at
107: events:            id, subscription_id, sequence, payload (JSON), status,
108:                    attempt_count, next_attempt_at, lease_until, last_error,
109:                    created_at, delivered_at
110: delivery_attempts: id, event_id, attempted_at, http_status, error, duration_ms
111: ```
112: 
113: Index on `(status, next_attempt_at)` for the dispatcher query.
114: 
115: ## 7. API
116: 
117: ### `POST /events`
118: 
119: Request:
120: 
121: ```json
122: { "subscription_id": "sub_123", "payload": { } }
123: ```
124: 
125: Response `202`:
126: 
127: ```json
128: { "event_id": "evt_...", "status": "pending" }
129: ```
130: 
131: ### `GET /events/{id}`
132: 
133: Response `200`: event status, `attempt_count`, `next_attempt_at`, `last_error`, and the attempt history.
134: 
135: ### Outgoing delivery (POST to customer endpoint)
136: 
137: - **Body:** `{ "event_id", "created_at", "sequence", "payload" }`
138: - **Headers:** `X-Event-Id`, `X-Delivery-Attempt`, `Content-Type: application/json`
139: 
140: ## 8. Non-functional requirements
141: 
142: - **Durability:** an event is stored before the 202 is returned.
143: - **Resilience:** one endpoint's failure must not stop deliveries to others.
144: - **Resource bounds:** fixed worker count, request timeouts, payload size limit.
145: - **Observability:** structured logs for each attempt, a `/healthz` endpoint, and simple counters (pending, delivered, failed).
146: - **Simplicity:** one-line run command, for example `go run ./cmd/server`.
147: 
148: ## 9. Test plan
149: 
150: A mock customer endpoint tool (a small Go HTTP server) with switchable behaviors: always 200, always 500, slow response beyond the timeout, intermittent failure, and "down for N minutes then recovers".
151: 
152: Key scenarios:
153: 
154: - **Happy path:** event delivered once, status `delivered`.
155: - **Endpoint down then recovers:** the event is eventually delivered with backoff visible in the attempt history.
156: - **Simulated long outage** (backoff and deadline times scaled down via config): other customers' deliveries stay timely, the breaker opens, and the stuck events end `failed` after the deadline.
157: - **Slow endpoint:** workers are released at the timeout, and the per-customer cap holds.
158: - **Crash mid-delivery:** after restart, the leased event is retried (duplicate is acceptable).
159: - **Unit tests** for backoff calculation and retry classification.
160: 
161: ## 10. Plan
162: 
163: | Block | Output |
164: |-------|--------|
165: | Setup, schema, subscriptions | Project skeleton, DB, migrations |
166: | Ingest and status API | `POST /events`, `GET /events/{id}` |
167: | Dispatcher, workers, retry and backoff | End-to-end delivery with retries |
168: | Per-customer cap and circuit breaker | Isolation during outages |
169: | Mock endpoint and tests | Scenarios from section 9 |

    RetryDeadline time.Duration // 24h
    BreakerThreshold int // 5
    BreakerBaseCoolDown time.Duration // e.g. 30s
    MaxPayloadBytes int64 // 1MB? PRD says payload size limit. Choose 256KB? Let's do 1MB default.
    LeaseDuration time.Duration // 30s? must be > RequestTimeout. e.g. 30s.
    PollInterval time.Duration // 500ms scheduler poll
    ShutdownTimeout
    SeedFile string // optional subscriptions seed json
}
```
Load from env vars with defaults (PRD defaults). Tests can scale down.

Clock injection: `type Clock func() time.Time`. Simpler: use `func() time.Time` throughout. For tests, we can use real clock with scaled config mostly; but for backoff unit tests, inject deterministic clock/rand. Jitter needs a rand source injectable too for deterministic tests: `retry.Backoff(attempt int, cfg, randFloat func() float64)`? Or package-level function with rng parameter. Design:

```go
package retry

// Backoff returns the delay before the given attempt number (1-based attempt that just failed).
func Delay(attempt int, cfg config.Retry, rnd func() float64) time.Duration
```

Hmm, config dependency: retry importing config is fine. But maybe define its own Params struct to avoid coupling: `retry.Params{Initial, Multiplier, Max, JitterFraction}`.

Classification:
```go
type Outcome int
const (Retry, Permanent)
func Classify(status int, err error) Decision```
Actually: `func ShouldRetry(statusCode int, err error) bool` — network error/timeout => retry; 5xx, 408, 429 => retry; other 4xx => no; 2xx handled before.

Also Retry-After parsing: `func RetryAfter(h http.Header, now time.Time) (time.Duration, bool)` supporting seconds and HTTP date.

### internal/store

SQLite with modernc.org/sqlite. Driver name: "sqlite". Use database/sql.

Schema:
```sql
CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS subscriptions (
  id TEXT PRIMARY KEY,
  customer_id TEXT NOT NULL,
  endpoint_url TEXT NOT NULL,
  created_at TIMESTAMP NOT NULL
);
CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS events (
  id TEXT PRIMARY KEY,
  subscription_id TEXT NOT NULL REFERENCES subscriptions(id),
  customer_id TEXT NOT NULL, -- denormalized for cap query? PRD schema doesn't include customer_id. Per-customer cap can join subscriptions. Keep join to avoid schema drift... but a join in claim query is fine. PRD data model lists specific columns; adding customer_id deviates. I'll join subscriptions on claim and cap query. Actually simpler: join.
  sequence INTEGER NOT NULL,
  payload TEXT NOT NULL,
  status TEXT NOT NULL,          -- pending | delivered | failed
  attempt_count INTEGER NOT NULL DEFAULT 0,
  next_attempt_at TIMESTAMP NOT NULL,
  lease_until TIMESTAMP,
  last_error TEXT,
  created_at TIMESTAMP NOT NULL,
  delivered_at TIMESTAMP
);
CREATE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS idx_events_status_next ON events(status, next_attempt_at);
CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS delivery_attempts (
  id INTEGER PRIMARY KEY AUTOINCREMENT,
  event_id TEXT NOT NULL REFERENCES events(id),
  attempted_at TIMESTAMP NOT NULL,
  http_status INTEGER,
  error TEXT,
  duration_ms INTEGER
);
```

Claim query (atomic, single transaction, sets lease):

```sql
BEGIN IMMEDIATE;
SELECT e.id FROM events e
 WHERE e.status='pending'
   AND e.next_attempt_at <= ?
   AND (e.lease_until IS NULL OR e.lease_until <= ?)
   AND (SELECT COUNT(*) FROM events x WHERE x.subscription_id = e.subscription_id
           AND x.status='pending'
           AND x.lease_until IS NOT NULL AND x.lease_until > ?) < ?   -- per-customer cap: count leased in-flight for same subscription... but cap is per-customer, not per-subscription. Customer may have multiple subscriptions. Use join to subscriptions for customer_id.
 ORDER BY e.next_attempt_at ASC, e.created_at ASC
 LIMIT 1;
UPDATE events SET lease_until = ? WHERE id = ?;
COMMIT;
```

Per-customer cap: count in-flight events for the same customer:
```sql
AND (SELECT COUNT(*) FROM events x JOIN subscriptions s2 ON s2.id = x.subscription_id
     WHERE s2.customer_id = s.customer_id
       AND x.lease_until IS NOT NULL AND x.lease_until > ?) < ?
```
with the outer e join subscriptions s. But we must count candidate's own customer's in-flight. Structure:

```sql
SELECT e.id FROM events e
  JOIN subscriptions s ON s.id = e.subscription_id
 WHERE e.status = 'pending'
   AND e.next_attempt_at <= @now
   AND (e.lease_until IS NULL OR e.lease_until <= @now)
   AND (SELECT COUNT(*) FROM events x
          JOIN subscriptions s2 ON s2.id = x.subscription_id
         WHERE s2.customer_id = s.customer_id
           AND x.lease_until IS NOT NULL AND x.lease_until > @now) < @cap
 ORDER BY e.next_attempt_at, e.created_at
 LIMIT 1
```
Run inside a single `BEGIN IMMEDIATE` transaction, then UPDATE lease. Modernc sqlite with database/sql: use `tx.ExecContext` — need to ensure BEGIN IMMEDIATE. database/sql default begins with BEGIN (deferred). With modernc driver, deferred transaction with a write at the end: two concurrent claims could both read then one fails with SQLITE_BUSY? Since single process, we can use a mutex around claim to be safe... but that defeats "atomic single transaction". Options:
- Use `BEGIN IMMEDIATE` explicitly: with database/sql, `db.BeginTx` issues BEGIN. You can exec "BEGIN IMMEDIATE" manually on a dedicated connection (`conn, _ := db.Conn(ctx); conn.ExecContext(ctx, "BEGIN IMMEDIATE")`). That works but bypasses db.Tx.
- Simpler and honest: use a single dedicated connection guarded... Hmm.

Actually modernc.org/sqlite: does it support `db.SetMaxOpenConns(1)`? Setting max open conns to 1 serializes all DB access through one connection — simple, correct, single-process. But then long transactions block API writes; fine at this scale. However, using max 1 connection with a claim transaction is fully atomic. Trade-off note: single connection serializes DB access; acceptable for single-process, small service.

But even with deferred BEGIN, with one connection there's no concurrency inside SQLite (single connection = no other transaction concurrently), so atomicity is guaranteed by SQLite itself plus serialization. Yet the "atomic claim in a single transaction" requirement is still satisfied.

I'd rather do it properly: use a dedicated `*sql.Conn` for claiming with `BEGIN IMMEDIATE`, or simply use `db.BeginTx` (deferred) — with MaxOpenConns(1) it's serialized anyway. Hmm, but deferred transactions in modernc: a transaction starts lazily? The driver executes BEGIN on Begin. Since only one connection exists, no other transaction can interleave. So atomic.

Plan: `db.SetMaxOpenConns(1)` with a comment explaining why (single process; serializes writes, avoids SQLITE_BUSY, keeps claim atomic without busy-wait logic). Hmm — but is one connection a bottleneck? For this project, no.

Actually, wait: worker does HTTP call outside transaction (good), API writes events — with 1 connection, an HTTP-delivery path never holds the connection during network I/O. Fine.

Alternatively SetMaxOpenConns(4) and use BEGIN IMMEDIATE via raw conn. Simpler: 1 connection. But there's a subtlety: modernc sqlite driver may have issues with concurrent use of the same conn — database/sql serializes access per conn, fine.

Let me reconsider: I'd like claim to be a single transaction even in a multi-conn future. I'll write claim using explicit BEGIN IMMEDIATE on a `*sql.Conn` from the pool... but with MaxOpenConns(1) that conn IS the pool. Combining both is belt and braces; simplest defensible: MaxOpenConns(1) + db.BeginTx.

Hmm, actually there's a risk: `database/sql` BeginTx with modernc driver issues "BEGIN" (deferred). Reads then write in deferred tx. With only one connection, no other SQLite transaction runs concurrently (same connection), so no upgrade deadlock possible. Good.

I'll go with MaxOpenConns(1) and note it.

Wait — one more consideration: WAL mode and busy_timeout. PRAGMA journal_mode=WAL; single conn anyway. I'll set `_pragma=busy_timeout(5000)` in DSN maybe. modernc DSN: `file:path?_pragma=busy_timeout(5000)&_pragma=journal_mode(WAL)`. Let's keep: DSN = "file:"+path+"?_pragma=busy_timeout(5000)&_pragma=journal_mode(WAL)&_pragma=foreign_keys(1)". Hmm foreign keys with references subscriptions(id) — fine.

Timestamps: store as RFC3339Nano UTC TEXT or Unix millis? Comparisons in SQL must be lexicographically consistent → RFC3339Nano UTC strings compare correctly if fixed width... RFC3339Nano drops trailing zeros in fractional seconds ("2026-01-01T00:00:00.1Z" vs "2026-01-01T00:00:00.12Z") — lexicographic comparison still works for same timezone offset since the integer second part is fixed-width and fractions compare digit by digit... careful: "00.1Z" vs "00.12Z": compare "00.1Z" vs "00.12Z": at index of 'Z' vs '2', 'Z'(0x5A) > '2'(0x32), so 0.1 > 0.12 lexicographically → wrong. So use fixed format: time.RFC3339 with milliseconds? Millis lose precision but fine? Attempts could be sub-ms apart in tests. Use UnixMilli integers stored as INTEGER — simplest and unambiguous. Or format with `2006-01-02T15:04:05.000000Z07:00` fixed width. I'll use a helper `formatTime(t)` = t.UTC().Format("2006-01-02T15:04:05.000000Z07:00") which is fixed-width and lexicographically sortable. And parse with time.Parse(time.RFC3339, s)? Parsing a fixed-width layout: I'd parse with the same layout string. Let's define in store:

```go
const timeLayout = "2006-01-02T15:04:05.000000Z07:00"
func formatTime(t time.Time) string { return t.UTC().Format(timeLayout) }
func parseTime(s string) (time.Time, error) { return time.Parse(timeLayout, s) }
```
Wait UTC format with "Z07:00" gives "Z" suffix; fixed width. Good.

Alternatively store Unix nano int64. Honestly integer nanos is simpler and avoids parsing issues: `t.UnixNano()`. Comparisons are numeric and correct. Downside: readability of DB rows. Nanos as INTEGER is fine, and it's year-1678..2262 safe. I'll go with UnixNano integers? Hmm, but PRD doesn't care. Fixed-width string is more human-readable when inspecting DB. I'll use the fixed-width string layout (readable + sortable) — with a comment why not RFC3339Nano.

Hmm, decision fatigue; choose fixed-width string.

Store API:

```go
type Store struct { db *sql.DB; now func() time.Time }

func Open(ctx, dbPath, clock) (*Store, error)  // runs migrations
func (s *Store) Close() error
func (s *Store) CreateSubscription(ctx, sub) error
func (s *Store) GetSubscription(ctx, id) (Subscription, error)
func (s *Store) ListSubscriptions(ctx) ([]Subscription, error)
func (s *Store) CreateEvent(ctx, subscriptionID string, payload []byte) (Event, error)  // assigns sequence per customer? PRD: per-customer sequence. Sequence: monotonic per customer. Compute as max(sequence)+1 for that customer within the insert transaction.
```
Per-customer sequence: 
```sql
INSERT INTO events (id, subscription_id, sequence, payload, status, attempt_count, next_attempt_at, created_at)
SELECT ?, ?, COALESCE(MAX(sequence),0)+1, ?, 'pending', 0, ?, ? FROM events e JOIN subscriptions s ON s.id=e.subscription_id WHERE s.customer_id = (SELECT customer_id FROM subscriptions WHERE id = ?)
```
That's awkward. Simpler: in a transaction: SELECT COALESCE(MAX(e.sequence),0)+1 FROM events e JOIN subscriptions s ON ... WHERE s.customer_id = ?; then INSERT. Two statements in one tx. Fine.

Edge: sequences never deleted, so monotonic. Good.

Also idempotency: `event_id` is the idempotency key for customers — producer may retry POST /events after losing response → duplicate event rows (each with new id). PRD says event_id is idempotency key for customer dedupe, not that the API dedupes. Keep simple: no API dedupe (note in DECISIONS).

Other store methods:
```go
func (s *Store) GetEvent(ctx, id) (Event, []Attempt, error)
func (s *Store) ListEvents(ctx, filter) ([]Event, error)

func (s *Store) ReleaseLease(...) // maybe part of reschedule
func (s *Store) Counts(ctx) (pending, delivered, failed int, err error)
func (s *Store) RecoverOnStartup(ctx, now) — clear expired leases? Actually claim already treats lease_until <= now as claimable. Expired lease rows are claimable directly. But status pending with expired lease → claimable. So no recovery needed. Optionally reset lease_until where lease_until <= now at startup — unnecessary. Good: crash safety falls out of claim query.
```

Hmm: also a lease safety: if the process crashes, lease expires → picked up again. That's handled.

Event struct fields: ID, SubscriptionID, CustomerID, Sequence, Payload (json.RawMessage), Status, AttemptCount, NextAttemptAt, LeaseUntil *time.Time, LastError, CreatedAt, DeliveredAt *time.Time.

### internal/retry

```go
package retry

type Class int
const (ClassRetry Class = iota; ClassFail)

func Classify(status int, err error) Class
// err != nil → retry (network/timeout); status 2xx handled by caller; 5xx/408/429 → retry; other 4xx → fail; other (3xx?) → PRD: "Any other response ... schedules a retry" (FR4 says any other response retries), but locked decision says other 4xx fail early. 3xx: treat as retry? Follow redirect? Don't follow redirects (default http.Client follows up to 10 — should disable? For webhook semantics, following redirects can leak... keep default? Simplest: use a client with CheckRedirect returning ErrUseLastResponse so 3xx is visible and classify as retry (not4xx). Hmm — PRD: retry on network errors, timeouts, 5xx, 408, 429; "Do not retry endlessly on permanent client errors such as 4xx other than 408/429." 3xx unspecified. Treat 3xx as permanent failure? Or retry? FR4 says "Any other response ... schedules a retry" except locked decision overrides for 4xx. For 3xx, FR4 default → retry (with reason "unexpected status"). But retrying a redirect forever until deadline is harmless-ish. Simpler: classify: 2xx → success (caller), 408/429/5xx → retry, other 4xx → fail, everything else (3xx, others) → retry per FR4. I'll document.

func Delay(attempt int, p Params, rnd func() float64) time.Duration
// attempt = number of attempts already made (>=1)
// base = Initial * Multiplier^(attempt-1), capped at Max
// jitter: factor = 1 + (rnd()*2-1)*JitterFraction → ±20%; clamp to [0, Max]... PRD: jitter ±20% of delay, and max delay 30min cap. Order: apply jitter then cap? Capping after jitter could reduce effective jitter at the cap; that's fine/standard. Also ensure >= some minimum (e.g., not negative — with20% jitter never negative).
```
Overflow: Multiplier^(attempt-1) can overflow float. Compute in float64 and cap: 
```go
base := float64(p.Initial) * math.Pow(p.Multiplier, float64(attempt-1))
if base > float64(p.Max) { base = float64(p.Max) }
```
math.Pow overflow → +Inf > Max → capped. Good.

rand: use injected `func() float64` in [0,1). Default `rand.Float64`. Jitter factor = 1 + (2*rnd()-1)*JitterFraction.

Retry-After:
```go
func RetryAfter(h http.Header, now time.Time) (time.Duration, bool)
```
parse seconds integer or HTTP date (RFC1123). Clamp: if <=0 → 0,true? If negative, ignore. Cap at Max delay? Respect but cap by remaining deadline (dispatcher handles deadline clamp).

### internal/breaker

Per-endpoint (per-subscription? per-endpoint URL. Use endpoint URL as key; subscriptions could share URL — PRD says per-endpoint). Key: endpoint URL.

```go
type Breaker struct {
  threshold int
  coolDownBase time.Duration  // grows: base * 2^(consecutiveFailures - threshold)
  mu sync.Mutex
  m map[string]*state
}
type state struct{ consecutive int; openedAt time.Time; coolDown time.Duration }

func (b *Breaker) Allow(endpoint string, now time.Time) bool  // false if in cool-down
func (b *Breaker) RecordSuccess(endpoint string)
func (b *Breaker) RecordFailure(endpoint string, now time.Time) // increments; if consecutive >= threshold → open with growing cool-down
```
Growth: after threshold-th failure, coolDown = base * 2^(consecutive - threshold), capped at maxCoolDown (e.g. 5min? PRD: "pause that endpoint for a cool-down that grows over time"). Config: BreakerCoolDown base30s, max 10min? Let's do base 30s, cap 15min? Hmm; keep: base 30s, max 10 min, growth exponent based on failures beyond threshold, reset on success.

On Allow when cool-down expired: should we half-open (allow one probe)? Simplest: when cool-down expires, allow traffic again; if failures continue, consecutive count keeps growing → longer cool-down. That's effectively "closed/open cycling" without explicit half-open. Note: do we reset `consecutive` when cool-down expires? If we don't, next failure immediately re-opens with bigger cool-down (good — endpoint still bad). If endpoint succeeds, reset. That's simple and defensible.

Where is Allow checked? In dispatcher before claiming: skip events whose endpoint breaker is open — but claim query doesn't know about breaker. Approach: worker claims an event, then checks breaker; if open, it should reschedule the event to `openedAt + coolDown` (release lease, set next_attempt_at = retryAt) and claim another. That avoids burning attempts (do not increment attempt_count or record attempt). This is clean: "instead of burning retries on every event."

Alternatively check breaker in claim query — can't (Go state). So worker-side check with reschedule. Need `Reschedule` without attempt recording: `s.RescheduleAt(ctx, id, t, reason?)`.

Careful: avoid infinite tight loop: worker claims → breaker open → reschedules → claims next → same endpoint... it will drain through the backlog quickly rescheduling all events for that endpoint to the cool-down time; that's O(n) statements, fine. But if many events for that endpoint keep being due... they're moved to future, so loop terminates. Potential edge: rescheduled to same future time repeatedly? No — after reschedule, next_attempt_at = breakerRetryAt (future), so it's not due.

If ALL events are for open breakers, workers spin claiming nothing? Claim returns none → poll sleep. But could workers busy-loop: claim event A (endpoint open) → reschedule → claim event B → ... until none due → return false → sleep poll interval. Fine.

Cap consideration: while rescheduling we hold/release lease. Reschedule clears lease.

### internal/delivery

```go
type Sender struct {
  client *http.Client  // with CheckRedirect ErrUseLastResponse, Timeout from config
  now func() time.Time
}
type Result struct { StatusCode int; Err error; Duration time.Duration; RetryAfter time.Duration; Body? }
func (s *Sender) Send(ctx, sub Endpoint, ev Event) Result
```
Body: `{"event_id":..., "created_at":..., "sequence":..., "payload":...}`. Headers X-Event-Id, X-Delivery-Attempt (attempt number = attempt_count+1), Content-Type: application/json.

Timeout: use per-request context timeout (config RequestTimeout) rather than client.Timeout (both fine; use context.WithTimeout).

Response body: read and discard limited (e.g., io.CopyN(io.Discard, resp.Body, 4KB)) then close — keeps connection reuse.

### internal/dispatcher

```go
type Dispatcher struct {
  store *store.Store
  sender *delivery.Sender
  breaker *breaker.Breaker
  cfg config.Config
  now func() time.Time
  rnd func() float64
  wg sync.WaitGroup
  counters *Counters (or metrics struct)
}
func (d *Dispatcher) Run(ctx) // starts N workers; blocks until ctx done and workers exit
```
Worker loop:
```go
for {
  select ctx.Done → return
  default:
  claimed, ok, err := claim...
  if err → log, sleep poll if !ok → sleep poll (with early wake? simple timer)
  process(claimed)
}
```
Processing:
1. Load subscription (endpoint URL, customer) — claim can return the joined endpoint directly to save a query. Return `ClaimedEvent{Event, EndpointURL, CustomerID}`.
2. breaker.Allow(endpoint, now): if false → store.RescheduleAt(id, breakerRetryAt) (no attempt), continue.
3. attempt := ev.AttemptCount + 1
4. result := sender.Send(ctx, ...)
5. store.RecordAttempt(...) always.
6. Classify:
   - success (2xx) → MarkDelivered; breaker.RecordSuccess; counters.delivered++
   - retry class → - if Retry-After present and >0 → delay = that (clamped)
     - else delay = retry.Delay(attempt, params, rnd)
     - next = created.Add... deadline: if now+delay > createdAt+RetryDeadline OR now >= createdAt+deadline → MarkFailed("retry deadline exceeded") — PRD: when deadline passes → failed. Better check first: if now >= createdAt + RetryDeadline → MarkFailed regardless (even before attempting? we already attempted). Then compute next; if next > deadline → clamp to... PRD says terminal failed after deadline, so if nextAttempt > deadline → MarkFailed? Or schedule at deadline then fail next time? Simpler and matching "when the deadline passes, the event moves to terminal failed": if now+delay >= deadline → MarkFailed now (record reason "retry deadline exceeded"). Hmm, that marks failed slightly before the deadline passes. Alternative: clamp next = min(now+delay, deadline) and at claim time check deadline → MarkFailed. That makes deadline check part of claim/processing: at start of process, if now >= createdAt+deadline → MarkFailed (terminal) without sending. That is exact: deadline passes → failed. I like: check before sending: if expired → MarkFailed (reason "retry deadline exceeded", clear lease). But then an event due exactly at deadline gets claimed, marked failed, attempt not counted. Good.

     Wait: but that costs a claim cycle. Fine.
     
     So order in process: (a) deadline check → MarkFailed; (b) breaker check → reschedule; (c) send; (d) classify.
     
     Hmm, breaker check should be before deadline? Doesn't matter much. Deadline first (cheap).
 - fail class (permanent 4xx) → MarkFailed with reason e.g. "permanent failure: status 400: ...".
 - breaker.RecordFailure on retry-class failure? PRD: breaker after 5 consecutive failures. Record failure for any non-2xx/error; success resets.
7. Counters.

Also note: attempt_count incremented where? On send: `Store.BeginAttempt` updates attempt_count = attempt_count+1 and lease? Or RecordAttempt increments attempt_count and inserts attempt row in one statement pair. I'll do: `RecordAttempt` inserts delivery_attempts and increments events.attempt_count, returns new count? We know count already (ev.AttemptCount+1). Keep simple: after send, `store.RecordAttempt(ctx, eventID, attempt, status, errMsg, dur)` → INSERT attempt row + UPDATE events SET attempt_count = attempt_count + 1, last_error = ?, lease_until = NULL (release lease as part of same op). Hmm but reschedule/delivered/failed also need lease release. Let's define:

- `store.CompleteAttempt(ctx, ev, att, next *NextAction)`: too clever. Instead separate calls in one tx each:
  - `RecordAttempt(ctx, eventID, att)` → INSERT + attempt_count+1 (lease untouched)
  - `MarkDelivered(ctx, eventID, now)` → status, delivered_at, lease_until=NULL
  - `Reschedule(ctx, eventID, nextAt, lastErr, now)` → next_attempt_at, last_error, lease_until=NULL
  - `MarkFailed(ctx, eventID, reason, now)` → status='failed', last_error, lease_until=NULL
  - `RescheduleOnly(ctx, eventID, nextAt)` (breaker path, no attempt record, no attempt_count) → next_attempt_at, lease_until=NULL. Could reuse Reschedule with lastErr = "circuit open"? Setting last_error to "circuit breaker open: ..." is informative and harmless. Then Reschedule covers both. Good — one method.

Multiple statements → wrap in transaction for atomicity. With MaxOpenConns(1), fine.

Counters: `type Counters struct { atomic.Int64 pending, delivered, failed, attempts, breakerOpens }`. Exposed at /healthz? PRD: "simple counters (pending, delivered, failed)". Implement `GET /healthz` returning JSON with counts from DB (COUNT queries) — simpler and accurate across restart. I'll compute counts via SQL in healthz handler, plus maybe in-process attempt counter. Simplest: healthz does3 COUNT queries. Keep a small in-memory counter for attempts? Not required. Do DB counts — accurate, no state drift. Log counters: structured logs per attempt via slog.

Structured logs: use `log/slog` (stdlib) with JSON or text handler. Text to stdout with attrs. Fine.

### internal/api

Routes with `http.ServeMux` (Go 1.22+ patterns: "POST /events", "GET /events/{id}", "GET /events", "GET /healthz", "POST /subscriptions").

Handlers:
- POST /events: read body with `http.MaxBytesReader` (MaxPayloadBytes + some slack for envelope). Decode `{subscription_id, payload}` where payload is raw JSON (json.RawMessage). Validate: subscription exists (404 unknown subscription... PRD says 4xx; use 404 or 400? "unknown subscription" → 404 Not Found is fine; or 400. I'll use 404 for unknown subscription, 400 for malformed JSON/missing field, 413 for oversized). Nothing stored on invalid.
  - Also validate payload is a JSON value (json.RawMessage with Valid check).
  - Persist → 202 `{"event_id":..., "status":"pending"}`.
- GET /events/{id}: 200 with event + attempts; 404 if missing.
- GET /events?subscription_id=&status=: list, optional filters; validate status value ∈ pending/delivered/failed (400 otherwise). Limit default 50, cap 500? Keep: `limit` param optional default 100. Hmm stretch — keep simple: filters only, LIMIT 200 fixed? I'll add limit param with default 100 max 500. Simpler: no limit param, SQL LIMIT 200. Keep it minimal: filters only + LIMIT 500? Let's do default 100, `limit` param honored with cap 500 — small code.
- POST /subscriptions: register `{customer_id, endpoint_url}` → 201 with id. Validate URL http/https, non-empty customer_id. PRD FR10 allows registering through a simple endpoint or seed config; we do both (seed file optional). Is the subscriptions endpoint in scope? AGENTS: "subscription registration (endpoint or seed config)" — in scope. Do both: seed file loaded at startup + POST /subscriptions.
- GET /healthz: counts + "ok".

API errors: JSON `{"error": "..."}`.

Validation must happen before storing (FR2) — yes.

### cmd/server

- load config (env)
- open store, migrate
- seed subscriptions from `SEED_FILE` (optional, JSON array)
- build dispatcher, start with signal-aware context (SIGINT/SIGTERM)
- start HTTP server; on signal: shutdown HTTP (stop accepting), cancel dispatcher ctx, wait with ShutdownTimeout.
Graceful shutdown semantics: "stop claiming, let in-flight requests finish within a timeout, then exit."

Order: signal → cancel ctx (dispatcher stops claiming; in-flight HTTP sends finish because their ctx... careful: worker sends use ctx derived from dispatcher ctx → cancel would abort in-flight requests. Need separate contexts: dispatcher's claim loop uses ctx; the send uses a context that is only cancelled after grace period. Implement: `Run(ctx)` where workers stop claiming when ctx done, but in-flight send continues until it completes or grace timeout; wait with `WaitGroup` bounded by grace timer.

Implementation: worker loop checks ctx before claim. process() uses `context.WithoutCancel(ctx)`? Hmm, WithTimeout(context.WithoutCancel(ctx), RequestTimeout) — that means sends aren't cancelled by shutdown but bounded by request timeout (10s) — that's fine and simpler: in-flight requests finish within request timeout, dispatcher waits up to ShutdownTimeout (e.g. 15s) for workers. But if a send is in progress and we want shutdown to abort it... PRD says let in-flight requests finish. So: send ctx = WithTimeout(WithoutCancel(ctx), RequestTimeout). Graceful: main waits for dispatcher with ShutdownTimeout; if exceeded, force exit (log). Since request timeout (10s) < shutdown timeout (15s), it works out.

Hmm: `context.WithoutCancel` exists since Go 1.21. Good. But then a stuck worker beyond timeout... request timeout bounds it. Fine.

main:
```go
ctx, stop := signal.NotifyContext(...)
go httpServer.ListenAndServe
<-ctx.Done()
shutdownCtx with timeout → server.Shutdown
dispatcher stops: run dispatcher in goroutine with its own ctx; cancel; wait channel with timeout
```

Simpler: dispatcher.Run(ctx) blocks in main goroutine:
```go
dctx, dcancel := context.WithCancel(context.Background())
go srv.ListenAndServe
workersDone := make(chan struct{})
go func(){ d.Run(dctx); close(workersDone) }()
<-rootCtx.Done()  // signal
dcancel()  // stop claiming
select { case <-workersDone: case <-time.After(cfg.ShutdownTimeout): log "forcing shutdown" }
shutdown http server
```

### cmd/mockendpoint

Small HTTP server with behavior switch via `-mode` flag or path. PRD: switchable behaviors: always 200, always 500, slow, intermittent, down for N minutes then recovers.

Design: flags: `-addr :9090`, `-mode ok|fail|slow|flaky|down`, `-delay 15s` (slow), `-down2m` (down duration), `-fail-rate 0.5`. Also allow runtime switching via `POST /_mode?mode=...`? Keep simple: flags + query/path-based? Tests need "down for N then recovers" — a `down` mode that returns 503 until start+downDuration then switches to ok automatically. And `flaky` alternating500/200 (deterministic alternation better than random for tests: first N fail then succeed? Use `-fail-count` : fail the first N requests then succeed). Deterministic is better for tests.

Modes:
- `ok`: always 200
- `fail`: always 500
- `slow`: sleep `-delay` then 200 (delay > request timeout → timeout)
- `flaky`: fail first `-fail-count` requests (default 2), then 200 — deterministic. Hmm "intermittent failure" → alternating: odd requests 500, even 200? For "down then recovers" we need fail-then-success. I'll implement: `flaky` = first `-fail-count` requests fail with 500, then 200.
- `down`: 503 for `-down` duration from server start, then 200. (mode `down` behaves like flaky with time-based recovery.)
Also record received requests (for tests to assert headers/body): log each request as JSON line; expose `GET /_requests` returning JSON array of received deliveries (helpful for tests asserting headers/body). That's a test aid, fine for a mock tool.

Tests can use httptest servers directly instead of the mock tool; but PRD requires a mock endpoint tool. I'll build `cmd/mockendpoint` and write scenario tests using httptest handlers (shared behavior via a small helper in internal/... hmm). To avoid duplication, implement the mock behaviors in a package `internal/mockendpoint` (or `internal/mock`) used by both `cmd/mockendpoint` and tests. Layout deviation? AGENTS suggested layout, "confirm before deviating" — adding internal/mockendpoint is a minor addition; I'll flag it in my report. Actually keep cmd/mockendpoint thin: flag parsing + `mockendpoint.New(mode, opts) http.Handler`. Good, tests import internal/mockendpoint.

Scenarios required:
1. happy path: event delivered once, status delivered.
2. endpoint down then recovers: use mock handler failing first N → event eventually delivered, attempt history shows backoff.
3. long outage with isolation: customer A endpoint always 500 with short deadline → ends failed; customer B deliveries stay timely; breaker opens (assert via breaker state or counters).
4. slow endpoint: request timeout releases workers; per-customer cap holds (assert max concurrent in-flight per customer ≤ cap via mock tracking concurrency).
5. crash mid-delivery: simulate by leasing an event (claim) without completing, then new dispatcher picks it up after lease expiry; duplicate acceptable (assert delivered and attempts ≥2 or that another delivery happened).

Time scaling: config with tiny values (initial delay 10ms, etc.). Use real clock with tiny durations → deterministic enough? "deterministic and fast; use injected clock or scaled config". Real clock with ms-scale is generally OK but can flake. For backoff test use injected rnd. For scenario tests, scaled config with real time and generous margins (e.g., poll5ms, deadline 2s). Risk of flakiness on CI... acceptable with margins.

Deterministic-ish approach: I could inject a clock that returns real time but tests only assert eventual outcomes within generous timeouts. I'll do that: poll/wait helpers with deadline (e.g., waitFor(t, 5*time.Second, cond)).

Crash test: create store, claim event (lease), close store; reopen new store + dispatcher → after lease expiry (e.g. 100ms) it gets delivered. That simulates crash (lease remains).Per-customer cap test: track concurrent in-flight per customer in the mock handler with atomic counters; slow handler with delay 100ms, cap 2, enqueue 6 events for one customer → assert observed max concurrent ≤ 2.

Isolation test: customer A endpoint always fails (breaker opens after 5), customer B ok. Assert B's events delivered quickly while A's pending; A's breaker open (query breaker.Allow or a getter); A's events eventually failed after deadline (deadline 500ms).

Also API tests: POST /events invalid (unknown sub, malformed JSON, oversized) → 4xx and store count 0; valid → 202 and GET returns pending.

Counters/healthz: maybe a test too.

Open:
```go
func Open(dbPath string, now func() time.Time) (*Store, error) {
	if now == nil { now = time.Now }
	dsn := fmt.Sprintf("file:%s?_pragma=busy_timeout(5000)&_pragma=journal_mode(WAL)&_pragma=foreign_keys(1)", dbPath)
	db, err := sql.Open("sqlite", dsn)
	...
	db.SetMaxOpenConns(1) // single process: one connection serializes access, so transactions never contend and claims stay atomic without busy-wait code.
	if _, err := db.Exec(schema); err != nil {...}
	return &Store{db: db, now: now}, nil
}
```
For in-memory tests: dbPath ":memory:" → DSN "file::memory:?cache=shared"? With MaxOpenConns(1), plain ":memory:" works (each new conn gets its own DB, but only 1 conn). Use dsn = dbPath when ":memory:". Let's handle: if dbPath == ":memory:" → "file::memory:?cache=shared"... careful. Simpler: tests use temp file path (t.TempDir()+"/test.db"). Schema (with PRD columns + indexes):

```sql
CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS subscriptions (
	id TEXT PRIMARY KEY,
	customer_id   TEXT NOT NULL,
	endpoint_url  TEXT NOT NULL,
	created_at    TEXT NOT NULL
);
CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS events (
	id               TEXT PRIMARY KEY,
	subscription_id  TEXT NOT NULL REFERENCES subscriptions(id),
	sequence         INTEGER NOT NULL,
	payload          TEXT NOT NULL,
	status           TEXT NOT NULL,
	attempt_count    INTEGER NOT NULL DEFAULT 0,
	next_attempt_at  TEXT NOT NULL,
	lease_until      TEXT,
	last_error       TEXT,
	created_at       TEXT NOT NULL,
	delivered_at     TEXT,
	lease_owner? -- not needed (single process)
);
CREATE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS idx_events_status_next ON events(status, next_attempt_at);
CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS delivery_attempts (
	id           INTEGER PRIMARY KEY AUTOINCREMENT,
	event_id     TEXT NOT NULL REFERENCES events(id),
	attempt      INTEGER NOT NULL,
	attempted_at TEXT NOT NULL,
	http_status  INTEGER,
	error        TEXT,
	duration_ms  INTEGER NOT NULL
);
```

Let me write the SQL:

```sql
SELECT e.id, e.subscription_id, e.sequence, e.payload, e.status, e.attempt_count,
       e.next_attempt_at, e.lease_until, e.last_error, e.created_at, e.delivered_at,
       s.customer_id, s.endpoint_url
  FROM events e
  JOIN subscriptions s ON s.id = e.subscription_id
 WHERE e.status = 'pending'
   AND e.next_attempt_at <= ?
   AND (e.lease_until IS NULL OR e.lease_until <= ?)
   AND (
 SELECT COUNT(*) FROM events x
          JOIN subscriptions s2 ON s2.id = x.subscription_id
         WHERE s2.customer_id = s.customer_id
           AND x.status = 'pending'
           AND x.lease_until IS NOT NULL
           AND x.lease_until > ?
   ) < ?
 ORDER BY e.next_attempt_at ASC, e.created_at ASC
 LIMIT 1
```
params: now, now, now, cap.

Then `UPDATE events SET lease_until = ? WHERE id = ? AND status='pending'` (guard status so we never lease a terminal row). Both inside one tx.

Note: because the SELECT and UPDATE run in the same transaction and SQLite serializes transactions on a single connection, claim is atomic.

Store CreateEvent:
```go
func (s *Store) CreateEvent(ctx context.Context, subscriptionID string, payload json.RawMessage) (Event, error) {
	var ev Event
	err := s.tx(ctx, func(tx *sql.Tx) error {
		var customerID string
		err := tx.QueryRowContext(ctx, `SELECT customer_id FROM subscriptions WHERE id = ?`, subscriptionID).Scan(&customerID)
		if errors.Is(err, sql.ErrNoRows) { return ErrNotFound }
		if err != nil { return fmt.Errorf("load subscription: %w", err) }

		var maxSeq sql.NullInt64
		if err := tx.QueryRowContext(ctx, `SELECT MAX(e.sequence) FROM events e JOIN subscriptions s ON s.id = e.subscription_id WHERE s.customer_id = ?`, customerID).Scan(&maxSeq); err != nil {...}
		seq := int64(1); if maxSeq.Valid && maxSeq.Int64 >=1 { seq = maxSeq.Int64 + 1 }

		now := s.now().UTC()
		ev = Event{ID: newID("evt_"), SubscriptionID: subscriptionID, CustomerID: customerID, Sequence: seq, Payload: payload, Status: StatusPending, NextAttemptAt: now, CreatedAt: now}
		_, err = tx.ExecContext(ctx, `INSERT INTO events (id, subscription_id, sequence, payload, status, attempt_count, next_attempt_at, lease_until, last_error, created_at, delivered_at) VALUES (?,?,?,?,?,0,?,NULL,NULL,?,NULL)`, ...)
		...
	})
	...
}
```
Note: a race on sequence under MaxOpenConns(1) — serialized, fine.

c.Retry.Deadline, err = envDur(\"RETRY_DEADLINE\", c.Retry.Deadline); err != nil {\n\t\treturn c, err\n\t}\n\n\tif c.Breaker.Threshold, err = envInt(\"BREAKER_THRESHOLD\", c.Breaker.Threshold); err != nil {\n\t\treturn c, err\n\t}\n\tif c.Breaker.CoolDown, err = envDur(\"BREAKER_COOLDOWN\", c.Breaker.CoolDown); err != nil {\n\t\treturn c, err\n\t}\n\tif c.Breaker.MaxCoolDown, err = Validate() error {\n\tswitch {\n\tcase c.Workers < 1:\n\t\treturn fmt.Errorf(\"WORKERS must be >= 1, got %d\", c.Workers)\n\tcase c.PerCustomerCap < 1:\n\t\treturn fmt.Errorf(\"PER_CUSTOMER_CAP must be >= 1, got %d\", c.PerCustomerCap)\n\tcase c.PollInterval <= 0:\n\t\treturn fmt.Errorf(\"POLL_INTERVAL must be > 0, got %s\", c.PollInterval)\n\tcase c.RequestTimeout <= 0:\n\t\treturn \"2006-01-02T15:04:05.000000Z07:00\"\n\nconst schema = `\nCREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS subscriptions (\n\tid           TEXT PRIMARY KEY,\n\tcustomer_id  TEXT NOT NULL,\n\tendpoint_url TEXT NOT TEXT,\n\tlast_error      TEXT,\n\tcreated_at      TEXT NOT NULL,\n\tdelivered_at    TEXT\n);\n\n-- Serves the dispatcher query: due events, oldest first.\nCREATE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS idx_events_status_next ON events(status, next_attempt_at);\n\nCREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS delivery_attempts (\n\tid           INTEGER PRIMARY KEY AUTOINCREMENT,\n\tevent_id     TEXT NOT NULL REFERENCES \"failed\"\n)\n\ntype Subscription struct {\n\tID          string    `json:\"id\"`\n\tCustomerID  string    `json:\"customer_id\"`\n\tEndpointURL string    `json:\"endpoint_url\"`\n\tCreatedAt   time.Time `json:\"created_at\"`\n}\n\ntype Event struct {\n\tID             string           `json:\"event_id\"`\n\tSubscriptionID string           `json:\"subscription_id\"`\n\t// CustomerID and EndpointURL come from the subscription join; the worker\n\t// needs them on every claim and a second round-trip is not worth it.\n\tCustomerID    string           `json:\"customer_id,omitempty\"`\n\tEndpointURL   string           `json:\"endpoint_url,omitempty\"`\n\tSequence      int64            `json:\"sequence\"`\n\tPayload       json.RawMessage  `json:\"payload\"`\n\tStatus        Status           `json:\"status\"`\n\tAttemptCount  int              `json:\"attempt_count\"`\n\tNextAttemptAt time.Time        `json:\"next_attempt_at\"`\n\tLeaseUntil    *time.Time       `json:\"lease_until,omitempty\"`\n\tLastError     string           `json:\"last_error,omitempty\"`\n\tCreatedAt     time.Time        `json:\"created_at\"`\n\tDeliveredAt   *time.Time       `json:\"delivered_at,omitempty\"`\n\tAttempts      []Attempt        `json:\"attempts,omitempty\"`\n}\n\ntype Attempt struct {\n\tID          int64     `json:\"id\"`\n\tEventID     string    `json:\"event_id\"`\n\tAttempt     int       `json:\"attempt\"`\n\tAttemptedAt time.Time `json:\"attempted_at\"`\n\tHTTPStatus  *int      `json:\"http_status,omitempty\"`\n\tError       string    `json:\"error,omitempty\"`\n\tDurationMS  int64     `json:\"duration_ms\"`\n}\n\ntype Counts struct {\n\tPending   int `json:\"pending\"`\n\tDelivered int `json:\"delivered\"`\n\tFailed    int `json:\"failed\"`\n}\n\ntype Store struct {\n\tdb  *sql.DB\n\tnow func() time.Time\n}\n\n// Open opens (or creates) the database, runs migrations and returns a Store.\n// now is the injected clock; nil means time.Now.\nfunc Open(dbPath string, now func() time.Time) (*Store, error) {\n\tif now == nil {\n\t\tnow = time.Now\n\t}\n\tdsn := fmt.Sprintf(\"file:%s?_pragma=busy_timeout(5000)&_pragma=journal_mode(WAL)&_pragma=foreign_keys(1)\", dbPath)\n\tdb, err := sql.Open(\"sqlite\", dsn)\n\tif err != nil {\n\t\treturn nil, fmt.Errorf(\"open sqlite: %w\", err)\n\t}\n\t// One connection serializes all access: transactions never contend, so a\n\t// claim (SELECT + UPDATE) is atomic without busy-wait/retry code. This is\n\t// safe because the service is a single process (a PRD non-goal is\n\t// multi-instance operation). Queries never hold the connection during\n\t// network I/O, so this does not stall the API or the workers.\n\tdb.SetMaxOpenConns(1)\n\n\tif _, err := db.Exec(schema); err != nil {\n\t\t_ = db.Close()\n\t\treturn nil, fmt.Errorf(\"migrate: %w\", err)\n\t}\n\treturn &Store{db: db, now: now}, nil\n}\n\nfunc (s *Store) Close() error { return s.db.Close() }\n\n// DB exposes the handle for tests only.\nfunc (s *Store) DB() *sql.DB { return s.db }\n\nfunc (s *Store) tx(ctx context.Context, fn func(*sql.Tx) error) error {\n\ttx, err := s.db.BeginTx(ctx, nil)\n\tif err != nil {\n\t\treturn fmt.Errorf(\"begin: %w\", err)\n\t}\n\tif err := fn(tx); err != nil {\n\t\tif rbErr := tx.Rollback(); rbErr != nil && !errors.Is(rbErr, sql.ErrTxDone) {\n\t\t\treturn fmt.Errorf(\"%v (rollback: %w)\", err, rbErr)\n\t\t}\n\t\treturn err\n\t}\n\tif err := tx.Commit(); there is no safe\n\t\t// fallback for an id that must be unique.\n\t\tpanic(fmt.Sprintf(\"generate id: %v\", err))\n\t}\n\treturn prefix + hex.EncodeToString(b[:])\n}\n\nfunc formatTime(t time.Time) string { return t.UTC().Format(timeLayout) }\n\nfunc parseTime(s string) (time.Time, error) {\n\tt, err := time.Parse(timeLayout, s)\n\tif err != nil {\n\t\treturn time.Time{}, fmt.Errorf(\"parse time %q: %w\", s, err)\n\t}\n\treturn t, nil\n}\n\n// scanEvent reads the 12 event columns produced by the claim/get queries.\nfunc scanEvent(row interface{ Scan(...any) error }) (Event, error) {\n\tvar (\n\t\tev           Event\n\t\tpayload      string\n\t\tnextAttempt  string\n\t\tcreatedAt    string\n\t\tleaseUntil   sql.NullString\n\t\tdeliveredAt  sql.NullString\n\t\tlastError    sql.NullString\n\t)\n\terr := row.Scan(\n\t\t&ev.ID, &ev.SubscriptionID, &ev.Sequence, &payload, &ev.Status,\n\t\t&ev.AttemptCount, &nextAttempt, &leaseUntil, &lastError, &createdAt,\n\t\t&deliveredAt, &ev.CustomerID, &ev.EndpointURL,\n\t)\n\tif errors.Is(err, sql.ErrNoRows) {\n\t\treturn Event{}, ErrNotFound\n\t}\n\tif err != nil {\n\t\treturn Event{}, fmt.Errorf(\"scan event: %w\", err)\n\t}\n\tif ev.NextAttemptAt, err = parseTime(nextAttempt); err != nil {\n\t\treturn Event{}, err\n\t}\n\tif ev.CreatedAt, err = parseTime(createdAt); err != nil {\n\t\treturn Event{}, err\n\t}\n\tev.Payload = json.RawMessage(payload)\n\tev.Status = Status(ev.Status)\n\tif leaseUntil.Valid && leaseUntil.String != \"\" {\n\t\tt, err := parseTime(leaseUntil.String)\n\t\tif err != nil {\n\t\t\treturn Event{}, err\n\t\t}\n\t\tev.LeaseUntil = &t\n\t}\n\tif deliveredAt.Valid && deliveredAt.String != \"\" {\n\t\tt, err := parseTime(deliveredAt.String)\n\t\tif err != nil {\n\t\t\treturn Event{}, err\n\t\t}\n\t\tev.DeliveredAt = &t\n\t}\n\tif lastError.Valid {\n\t\tev.LastError = lastError.String\n\t}\n\treturn ev, nil\n}\n\nconst eventColumns = `e.id, e.subscription_id, e.sequence, e.payload, e.status,\n\te.attempt_count, e.next_attempt_at, e.lease_until, e.last_error, e.created_at,\n\te.delivered_at, s.customer_id, s.endpoint_url`\n"

The row\n// exists before the API answers 202 (durability requirement).\n// Sequence is monotonic per customer so consumers can reorder deliveries.\nfunc (s *Store) CreateEvent(ctx context.Context, subscriptionID string, payload json.RawMessage) (Event, error) {\n\tvar ev Event\n\terr := s.tx(ctx, func(tx *sql.Tx) error {\n\t\tvar customerID string\n\t\terr := tx.QueryRowContext(ctx,\n\t\t\t`SELECT customer_id FROM subscriptions WHERE id = ?`, subscriptionID,\n\t\t).Scan(&customerID)\n\t\tif errors.Is(err, sql.ErrNoRows) {\n\t\t\treturn ErrNotFound\n\t\t}\n\t\tif err != nil {\n\t\t\treturn fmt.Errorf(\"load subscription: %w\", err)\n\t\t}\n\n\t\tvar maxSeq sql.NullInt64\n\t\tif err := tx.QueryRowContext(ctx,\n\t\t\t`SELECT MAX(e.sequence) FROM events e\n\t\t\t   JOIN subscriptions s ON s.id = e.subscription_id\n\t\t\t  WHERE s.customer_id = ?`, customerID,\n\t\t).Scan(&maxSeq); err != nil {\n\t\t\treturn fmt.Errorf(\"load next sequence: %w\", err)\n\t\t}\n\t\tseq := int64(1)\n\t\tif maxSeq.Valid && maxSeq.Int64 >= seq {\n\t\t\tseq = maxSeq.Int64 + 1\n\t\t}\n\n\t\tnow := s.now().UTC()\n\t\tev = Event{\n\t\t\tID:             newID(\"evt_\"),\n\t\t\tSubscriptionID: subscriptionID,\n\t\t\tCustomerID:     customerID,\n\t\t\tSequence:       seq,\n\t\t\tPayload:        payload,\n\t\t\tStatus:         StatusPending,\n\t\t\tNextAttemptAt:  now,\n\t\t\tCreatedAt:      now,\n\t\t}\n\t\t_, err = tx.ExecContext(ctx,\n\t\t\t`INSERT INTO events (id, subscription_id, sequence, payload, status,\n\t\t\t\tattempt_count, next_attempt_at, lease_until, last_error, created_at, delivered_at)\n\t\t\t\tVALUES (?, ?, ?, ?, ?, 0, ?, NULL, NULL, ?, NULL)`,\n\t\t\tev.ID, ev.SubscriptionID, ev.Sequence, string(ev.Payload), ev.Status,\n\t\t\tformatTime(ev.NextAttemptAt), formatTime(ev.CreatedAt),\n\t\t)\n\t\tif err != nil {\n\t\t\treturn fmt.Errorf(\"insert event: %w\", err)\n\t\t}\n\t\treturn nil\n\t})\n\tif err != nil {\n\t\treturn Event{}, err\n\t}\n\treturn ev, nil\n}\n\n// GetEvent returns the event with its full attempt history (FR7).\nfunc (s *Store) GetEvent(ctx context.Context, id string) (Event, error) {\n\tev, err := scanEvent(s.db.QueryRowContext(ctx,\n\t\t`SELECT `+eventColumns+` FROM events e JOIN subscriptions s ON s.id = e.subscription_id WHERE e.id = ?`, id))\n\tif err != nil {\n\t\treturn Event{}, err\n\t}\n\tattempts, err := s.listAttempts(ctx, id)\n\tif err != nil {\n\t\treturn Event{}, err\n\t}\n\tev.Attempts = attempts\n\treturn ev, nil\n}\n\nfunc (s *Store) listAttempts(ctx context.Context, eventID string) ([]Attempt, error) {\n\trows, err := s.db.QueryContext(ctx,\n\t\t`SELECT id, event_id, attempt, attempted_at, http_status, error, duration_ms\n\t\t   FROM delivery_attempts WHERE event_id = ? ORDER BY id`, eventID)\n\tif err != nil {\n\t\treturn nil, fmt.Errorf(\"list attempts: %w\", err)\n\t}\n\tdefer rows.Close()\n\n\tvar out []Attempt\n\tfor rows.Next() {\n\t\tvar (\n\t\t\ta           Attempt\n\t\t\tattemptedAt string\n\t\t\tstatus      sql.NullInt64\n\t\t)\n\t\tif err := rows.Scan(&a.ID, &a.EventID, &a.Attempt, &attemptedAt, &status, &a.Error, &a.DurationMS); err != nil {\n\t\t\treturn nil, fmt.Errorf(\"scan attempt: %w\", err)\n\t\t}\n\t\tif a.AttemptedAt, err = parseTime(attemptedAt); err != nil {\n\t\t\treturn nil, err\n\t\t}\n\t\tif status.Valid {\n\t\t\ts := int(status.Int64)\n\t\t\ta.HTTPStatus = &s\n\t\t}\n\t\tout = append(out, a)\n\t}\n\tif err := rows.Err(); err != nil {\n\t\treturn nil, fmt.Errorf(\"iterate attempts: %w\", err)\n\t}\n\treturn out, nil\n}\n\ntype ListFilter struct {\n\tSubscriptionID string\n\tCustomerID     string\n\tStatus         Status\n\tLimit          int\n}\n\n// ListEvents supports FR8 (inspection listing). Conditions are appended as\n// fixed SQL fragments with bound placeholders; user input never enters the SQL\n// text.\nfunc (s *Store) ListEvents(ctx context.Context, f ListFilter) ([]Event, error) {\n\tvar (\n\t\tconds []string\n\t\targs  []any\n\t)\n\tif f.SubscriptionID != \"\" {\n\t\tconds = append(conds, \"e.subscription_id = ?\")\n\t\targs = append(args, f.SubscriptionID)\n\t}\n\tif f.CustomerID != \"\" {\n\t\tconds = append(conds, \"s.customer_id = ?\")\n\t\targs = append(args, f.CustomerID)\n\t}\n\tif f.Status != \"\" {\n\t\tconds = append(conds, \"e.status = ?\")\n\t\targs = append(args, f.Status)\n\t}\n\tquery := `SELECT ` + eventColumns + ` FROM events e JOIN subscriptions s ON s.id = e.subscription_id`\n\tif len(conds) > 0 {\n\t\tquery += \" WHERE \" + strings.Join(conds, \" AND \")\n\t}\n\tquery += \" ORDER BY e.created_at DESC, e.id DESC LIMIT ?\"\n\tlimit := f.Limit\n\tif limit <= 0 {\n\t\tlimit = 100\n\t}\n\targs = append(args, limit)\n\n\trows, err := s.db.QueryContext(ctx, query, args...)\n\tif err != nil {\n\t\treturn nil, fmt.Errorf(\"list events: %w\", err)\n\t}\n\tdefer rows.Close()\n\n\tvar out []Event\n\tfor rows.Next() {\n\t\tev, err := scanEvent(rows)\n\t\tif err != nil {\n\t\t\treturn nil, err\n\t\t}\n\t\tout = append(out, ev)\n\t}\n\tif err := rows.Err(); err != nil {\n\t\treturn nil, fmt.Errorf(\"iterate events: %w\", err)\n\t}\n\treturn out, nil\n}\n\n// RecordAttempt always writes a delivery_attempts row (status, error,\n// duration) and bumps attempt_count. It does not change status or the lease:\n// the caller decides delivered / retry / failed immediately afterwards.\nfunc (s *Store) RecordAttempt(ctx context.Context, eventID string, a Attempt) error {\n\tvar status any\n\tif a.HTTPStatus != nil {\n\t\tstatus = *a.HTTPStatus\n\t}\n\terr := s.tx(ctx, func(tx *sql.Tx) error {\n\t\tif _, err := tx.ExecContext(ctx,\n\t\t\t`INSERT INTO delivery_attempts (event_id, attempt, attempted_at, http_status, error, duration_ms)\n\t\t\t\tVALUES (?, ?, ?, ?, ?, ?)`,\n\t\t\teventID, a.Attempt, formatTime(a.AttemptedAt), status, a.Error, a.DurationMS,\n\t\t); err != nil {\n\t\t\treturn fmt.Errorf(\"insert attempt: %w\", err)\n\t\t}\n\t\tres, err := tx.ExecContext(ctx,\n\t\t\t`UPDATE events SET attempt_count = attempt_count + 1 WHERE id = ?`, eventID)\n\t\tif err != nil {\n\t\t\treturn fmt.Errorf(\"bump attempt_count: %w\", err)\n\t\t}\n\t\tn, err := res.RowsAffected()\n\t\tif err != nil {\n\t\t\treturn fmt.Errorf(\"rows affected: %w\", err)\n\t\t}\n\t\tif n == 0 {\n\t\t\treturn ErrNotFound\n\t\t}\n\t\treturn nil\n\t})\n\treturn err\n}\n\n// MarkDelivered releases the lease and records the terminal success state (FR4).\nfunc (s *Store) MarkDelivered(ctx context.Context, eventID string, at time.Time) error {\n\treturn s.updateTerminal(ctx, eventID,\n\t\t`UPDATE events SET status = ?, delivered_at = ?, lease_until = NULL WHERE id = ? AND status = ?`,\n\t\tStatusDelivered, formatTime(at), eventID, StatusPending)\n}\n\n// MarkFailed moves the event to its terminal state (FR6). The row and its\n// attempts are never deleted, so it can be replayed manually later.\nfunc (s *Store) MarkFailed(ctx context.Context, eventID, reason string, at time.Time) error {\n\treturn s.updateTerminal(ctx, eventID,\n\t\t`UPDATE events SET status = ?, last_error = ?, lease_until = NULL, next_attempt_at = ? WHERE id = ? AND status = ?`,\n\t\tStatusFailed, reason, formatTime(at), eventID, StatusPending)\n}\n\n// Reschedule puts the event back in the queue at nextAt and releases the lease,\n// so no worker is parked between attempts. lastErr is recorded for FR7.\nfunc (s *Store) Reschedule(ctx context.Context, eventID string, nextAt time.Time, lastErr string) error {\n\terr := s.tx(ctx, func(tx *sql.Tx) error {\n\t\tres, err := tx.ExecContext(ctx,\n\t\t\t`UPDATE events SET next_attempt_at = ?, last_error = ?, lease_until = NULL\n\t\t\t  WHERE id = ? AND status = ?`,\n\t\t\tformatTime(nextAt), lastErr, eventID, StatusPending)\n\t\tif err != nil {\n\t\t\treturn fmt.Errorf(\"reschedule event: %w\", err)\n\t\t}\n\t\treturn requireRow(res)\n\t})\n\treturn err\n}\n\nfunc (s *Store) updateTerminal(ctx context.Context, eventID, query string, args ...any) error {\n\terr := s.tx(ctx, func(tx *sql.Tx) error {\n\t\tres, err := tx.ExecContext(ctx, query, args...)\n\t\tif err != nil {\n\t\t\treturn fmt.Errorf(\"update event %s: %w\", eventID, err)\n\t\t}\n\t\treturn requireRow(res)\n\t})\n\treturn err\n}\n\nfunc requireRow(res sql.Result) error {\n\tn, err := res.RowsAffected()\n\tif err != nil {\n\t\treturn fmt.Errorf(\"rows affected: %w\", err)\n\t}\n\tif n == 0 {\n\t\t// The event already left `pending` (e.g. another path finished it);\n\t\t// returning ErrNotFound lets the caller log it instead of double-applying.\n\t\treturn fmt.Errorf(\"event not pending: %w\", ErrNotFound)\n\t}\n\treturn nil\n}\n\n// Counts powers the /healthz counters (pending, delivered, failed).\nfunc (s *Store) Counts(ctx context.Context) (Counts, error) {\n\trows, err := s.db.QueryContext(ctx, `SELECT status, COUNT(*) FROM events GROUP BY status`)\n\tif err != nil {\n\t\treturn Counts{}, fmt.Errorf(\"count events: %w\", err)\n\t}\n\tdefer rows.Close()\n\n\tvar c Counts\n\tfor rows.Next() {\n\t\tvar (\n\t\t\tstatus Status\n\t\t\tn      int\n\t\t)\n\t\tif err := rows.Scan(&status, &n); err != nil {\n\t\t\treturn Counts{}, fmt.Errorf(\"scan count: %w\", err)\n\t\t}\n\t\tswitch status {\n\t\tcase StatusPending:\n\t\t\tc.Pending = n\n\t\tcase StatusDelivered:\n\t\t\tc.Delivered = n\n\t\tcase StatusFailed:\n\t\t\tc.Failed = n\n\t\t}\n\t}\n\tif err := rows.Err(); err != nil {\n\t\treturn Counts{}, fmt.Errorf(\"iterate counts: %w\", err)\n\t}\n\treturn c, nil\n}\n",

package retry

import (

// Decision of the retry classifier for one attempt.
type Decision int

const (
	// DecisionRetry: transient failure, schedule another attempt.
	DecisionRetry Decision = iota
	// DecisionFail: permanent failure, move to terminal `failed` now.
	DecisionFail
)

// Classify implements the locked retry policy:
// network errors and timeouts retry; 5xx, 408 and 429 retry; every other 4xx
// is permanent so we stop hammering a caller that will never accept the event.
// Anything else (3xx, 1xx) retries per FR4 "any other response schedules a retry".
func Classify(status int, err error) Decision {
	if err != nil {
		return DecisionRetry // network error or timeout: outcome unknown
	}
	switch {
	case status >= 200 && status < 300:
		return DecisionFail??}
}

	OutcomeRetry                    // transient

- 2xx → OutcomeSuccess
- 5xx, 408, 429 → OutcomeRetry

Params & Delay:

```go
type Params struct {
	Initial time.Duration
	Multiplier float64
	Jitter float64
	Max time.Duration
}

// Delay returns the backoff before the next attempt after `attempts` attempts
// have already been made (attempts >= 1): initial * multiplier^(attempts-1),
// jittered by ±Jitter, capped at Max. rnd must return [0,1).
func Delay(attempts int, p Params, rnd func() float64) time.Duration {
	if attempts < 1 { attempts = 1 }
	base := float64(p.Initial) * math.Pow(p.Multiplier, float64(attempts-1))
	if base > float64(p.Max) || math.IsInf(base,0) || math.IsNaN(base) { base = float64(p.Max) }
	d := base
	if p.Jitter > 0 && rnd != nil {
		d = base * (1 + (2*rnd()-1)*p.Jitter)
	}
	if d < 0 { d = 0 }
	if d > float64(p.Max) { d = float64(p.Max) }
	return time.Duration(d)
}
```
Note: capping after jitter means the top of the jitter range gets compressed at the cap — document as intentional so delay never exceeds Max.

RetryAfter:
```go
// RetryAfter parses the Retry-After header (seconds or HTTP date) relative to now.
func RetryAfter(h http.Header, now time.Time) (time.Duration, bool) {
	v := strings.TrimSpace(h.Get("Retry-After"))
	if v == "" { return 0, false }
	if secs, err := strconv.Atoi(v); err == nil {
		if secs < 0 { return 0, false }
		return time.Duration(secs) * time.Second, true
	}
	if t, err := http.ParseTime(v); err == nil {
		d := t.Sub(now)
		if d < 0 { d = 0 }
		return d, true
	}
	return 0, false
}
```

package delivery

type Sender struct {
	client *http.Client
	timeout time.Duration
	now func() time.Time
}

type Result struct {
	StatusCode int
	Err error        // transport error (network/timeout)
	Duration time.Duration
	RetryAfter time.Duration
	HasRetryAfter bool
	At time.Time
}

func NewSender(timeout time.Duration, now func() time.Time) *Sender {
	return &Sender{
		client: &http.Client{
			Timeout: timeout, // belt: the context below is the primary bound
			// Do not follow redirects: a 3xx is an answer we must classify and
			// record, and following could silently POST the payload elsewhere.
			CheckRedirect: func(*http.Request, []*http.Request) error { return http.ErrUseLastResponse },
		},
		timeout: timeout,
		now: now,
	}
}

func (s *Sender) Send(ctx context.Context, endpoint string, ev store.Event, attempt int) Result {
	// WithoutCancel: shutdown must not abort an in-flight delivery; the
	// request timeout still bounds it (graceful shutdown lets requests finish).
	ctx, cancel := context.WithTimeout(context.WithoutCancel(ctx), s.timeout)
	defer cancel()

	body, err := json.Marshal(deliveryBody{EventID: ev.ID, CreatedAt: ev.CreatedAt, Sequence: ev.Sequence, Payload: ev.Payload})
	...
	req, err := http.NewRequestWithContext(ctx, http.MethodPost, endpoint, bytes.NewReader(body))
	if err != nil { return Result{Err: err, ...} }
	req.Header.Set("X-Event-Id", ev.ID)
	req.Header.Set("X-Delivery-Attempt", strconv.Itoa(attempt))
	req.Header.Set("Content-Type", "application/json")

	start := s.now()
	resp, err := s.client.Do(req)
	dur := s.now().Sub(start)
	if err != nil { return Result{Err: err, Duration: dur, At: start} }
	defer resp.Body.Close()
	// Drain a bounded amount so the connection can be reused.
	_, _ = io.CopyN(io.Discard, resp.Body, 4096)
	ra, has := retry.After? -> avoid import cycle: retry package importing nothing from delivery; delivery importing retry is fine (retry doesn't import delivery).
	return Result{StatusCode: resp.StatusCode, Duration: dur, At: start, RetryAfter: ra, HasRetryAfter: has}
}
```

```go
package dispatcher

type Dispatcher struct {
	store *store.Store
	sender *delivery.Sender
	breaker *breaker.Breaker
	cfg config.Config
	log *slog.Logger
	now func() time.Time
	rnd func() float64
	inflight sync.WaitGroup  // careful naming
}

func New(st, sender, brk, cfg, log, now, rnd) *Dispatcher

// Run blocks until ctx is cancelled and all workers have finished their
// current event (graceful shutdown: stop claiming, finish in flight).
func (d *Dispatcher) Run(ctx context.Context) {
	var wg sync.WaitGroup
	for i := 0; i < d.cfg.Workers; i++ {
		wg.Add(1)
		go func(id int){ defer wg.Done(); d.worker(ctx, id) }(i)
	}
	wg.Wait()
}

func (d *Dispatcher) worker(ctx context.Context, id int) {
	for {
		if ctx.Err() != nil { return }  // stop claiming on shutdown
		ev, ok, err := d.claim(ctx)
		if err != nil {
			if ctx.Err() != nil { return }
			d.log.Error("claim event", "worker", id, "error", err)
	}
		if !ok {
			if !sleep(ctx, d.cfg.PollInterval) { return }
			continue
		}
		d.process(ctx, id, ev)
	}
}
```

func (d *Dispatcher) claim(ctx) (store.Event, bool, error) {
	now := d.now()
	leaseUntil := now.Add(d.cfg.LeaseDuration)
	return d.store.ClaimDue(ctx, now, leaseUntil, d.cfg.PerCustomerCap)
}

func attemptError(res delivery.Result) string {
	if res.Err != nil { return res.Err.Error() }
	if res.StatusCode < 200 || res.StatusCode > 299 {
		s := fmt.Sprintf("HTTP %d", res.StatusCode)
		if res.BodySnippet != "" { s += ": " + res.BodySnippet }
		return s
	}
	return ""
}
