```go
func sleep(ctx context.Context, d time.Duration) bool {
	t := time.NewTimer(d)
	defer t.Stop()
	select {
	case <-ctx.Done(): return false
	case <-t.C: return true
	}
}
```

One important issue: workers that claim nothing sleep PollInterval (250ms default). After processing an event they immediately loop and try to claim again — that's fine (claim is a cheap query).

But busy-claim when there are many events with open breakers: covered earlier.

Another important consideration: **event due check vs. now injection**: uses real time from d.now.

Graceful shutdown semantics: Run returns when all workers exit. Workers exit when ctx done and not in process. In-flight process uses ctx for store ops — store ops with cancelled ctx would fail! If ctx cancelled while the worker is mid-process, `d.store.RecordAttempt(ctx...)` would return context.Canceled → attempt not recorded → but event has a lease that expires → redelivered later. Acceptable but noisy. Better: use a separate context for the processing steps that isn't cancelled by shutdown: `procCtx := context.WithoutCancel(ctx)` bounded by lease duration? The send already uses WithoutCancel + RequestTimeout. So: in worker, `d.process(context.WithoutCancel(ctx), ...)`. That means on shutdown, in-flight events complete (bounded by request timeout), then workers exit. Run waits for them. main waits ShutdownTimeout then proceeds.

Good. Note the lease guarantees correctness if we exceed ShutdownTimeout.

Breaker API:
```go
package breaker

type Breaker struct {
	threshold int
	coolDown time.Duration
	maxCoolDown time.Duration
	mu sync.Mutex
	states map[string]*state
}
type state struct {
	consecutive int
	openedAt time.Time   // when cool-down started
	coolDown time.Duration // current cool-down length
}

func New(threshold int, coolDown, maxCoolDown time.Duration) *Breaker

// Allow reports whether a request to endpoint may be attempted now.
func (b *Breaker) Allow(endpoint string, now time.Time) bool {
	b.mu.Lock(); defer b.mu.Unlock()
	st := b.states[endpoint]
	if st == nil || st.coolDown == 0 { return true }
	return !now.Before(st.openedAt.Add(st.coolDown))
}

// RetryAt returns when Allow becomes true again (now if closed).
func (b *Breaker) RetryAt(endpoint string, now time.Time) time.Time

// RecordSuccess resets.
func (b *Breaker) RecordSuccess(endpoint string) { ... st.consecutive = 0; st.coolDown = 0 }

// RecordFailure counts; on reaching threshold (or beyond) it opens with a cool-down that doubles per consecutive failure, capped.
func (b *Breaker) RecordFailure(endpoint string, now time.Time) (opened bool) {
	st := ...
	st.consecutive++
	if st.consecutive < b.threshold { return false }
	// failures beyond threshold: consecutive == threshold → 1×coolDown, threshold+1 → 2×...
shift := st.consecutive - b.threshold
	cd := b.coolDown << shift  (careful overflow) → compute via loop/float
	if cd > b.maxCoolDown { cd = b.maxCoolDown }
	st.openedAt = now; st.coolDown = cd
	return true
}
```
Overflow: shift with int duration; guard with loop multiplying and cap:
```go
cd := b.coolDown
for i := 0; i < shift && cd < b.maxCoolDown; i++ { cd *= 2 }
if cd > b.maxCoolDown { cd = b.maxCoolDown }
```
Note: when Allow returns true after cool-down expiry (half-open), we keep coolDown set (state remains "opened" but expired). If failure happens again → consecutive++ → longer cool-down, openedAt=now. If success → reset. Also if a request is allowed after expiry and no success/failure yet, it stays expired → allowed. Good.

{
  "content": "// Package retry implements the locked retry policy: classification of an\n// attempt outcome and the exponential backoff with jitter.\npackage retry\n\nimport (\n\t\"math\"\n\t\"net/http\"\n\t\"strconv\"\n\t\"strings\"\n\t\"time\"\n)\n\n// Outcome of a single delivery attempt.\ntype Outcome int\n\nconst (\n\t// OutcomeSuccess: 2xx, the event is delivered (FR4).\n\tOutcomeSuccess Outcome = iota\n\t// OutcomeRetry: transient, schedule another attempt (FR5).\n\tOutcomeRetry\n\t// OutcomeFail: permanent, move to terminal `failed` now.\n\tOutcomeFail\n)\n\n// Classify decides what happens after one attempt.\n//\n//   - transport error (network failure, timeout): retry — we cannot know\n//     whether the receiver processed it, and at-least-once demands trying again;\n//   - 2xx: success;\n//   - 5xx, 408, 429: retry, these are explicitly transient;\n//   - any other 4xx: fail early — retrying a request the receiver calls\n//     invalid only burns quota until the deadline;\n//   - everything else (1xx/3xx): retry, per FR4 \"any other response schedules\n//     a retry\". Redirects are not followed, so a 3xx lands here and is visible\n//     in the attempt history.\nfunc Classify(status int, err error) Outcome {\n\tif err != nil {\n\t\treturn OutcomeRetry\n\t}\n\tswitch {\n\tcase status >= 200 && status < 300:\n\t\treturn OutcomeSuccess\n\tcase status == http.StatusRequestTimeout,\n\t\tstatus == http.StatusTooManyRequests,\n\t\tstatus >= 500:\n\t\treturn OutcomeRetry\n\tcase status >= 400 && status < 500:\n\t\treturn OutcomeFail\n\tdefault:\n\t\treturn OutcomeRetry\n\t}\n}\n\n// Params are the tunable backoff parameters (PRD defaults live in config).\ntype Params struct {\n\tInitial    time.Duration\n\tMultiplier float64\n\t// Jitter is the ± fraction of the delay, e.g. 0.2 for ±20%.\n\tJitter float64\n\tMax    time.Duration\n}\n\n// Delay returns how long to wait before the next attempt, given the number of\n// attempts already made (>= 1):\n//\n//\tbase    = Initial * Multiplier^(attempts-1), capped at Max\n//\tdelayed = base * (1 + (2*rnd()-1)*Jitter), then capped at Max again\n//\n// The second cap means a jittered delay never exceeds Max; at the cap the\n// jitter range is therefore compressed, which is the lesser evil compared to\n// sleeping past the configured maximum. rnd must return a value in [0,1); a\n// nil rnd means no jitter (useful in tests).\nfunc Delay(attempts int, p Params, rnd func() float64) time.Duration {\n\tif attempts < 1 {\n\t\tattempts = 1\n\t}\n\tbase := float64(p.Initial) * math.Pow(p.Multiplier, float64(attempts-1))\n\t// math.Pow can overflow to +Inf for large attempt counts; the cap handles it.\n\tif math.IsNaN(base) || math.IsInf(base, 0) || base > float64(p.Max) {\n\t\tbase = float64(p.Max)\n\t}\n\tdelay := base\n\tif p.Jitter > 0 && rnd != nil {\n\t\tdelay = base * (1 + (2*rnd()-1)*p.Jitter)\n\t}\n\tif delay < 0 {\n\t\tdelay = 0\n\t}\n\tif delay > float64(p.Max) {\n\t\tdelay = float64(p.Max)\n\t}\n\treturn time.Duration(delay)\n}\n\n// RetryAfter parses the Retry-After header (delay in seconds or an HTTP date)\n// and returns the wait relative to now. ok is false when the header is absent\n// or unusable, in which case the caller falls back to exponential backoff.\nfunc RetryAfter(h http.Header, now time.Time) (delay time.Duration, ok bool) {\n\tv := strings.TrimSpace(h.Get(\"Retry-After\"))\n\tif v == \"\" {\n\t\treturn 0, false\n\t}\n\tif secs, err := strconv.Atoi(v); err == nil {\n\t\tif secs < 0 {\n\t\t\treturn 0, false\n\t\t}\n\t\treturn time.Duration(secs) * time.Second, true\n\t}\n\tif t, err := http.ParseTime(v); err == nil {\n\t\td := t.Sub(now)\n\t\tif d < 0 {\n\t\t\td = 0\n\t\t}\n\t\treturn d, true\n\t}\n\treturn 0, false\n}\n",

}

{
  "content": "// Package breaker is a per-endpoint circuit breaker: after K consecutive\n// failures the endpoint is paused for a growing cool-down instead of burning\n// retries on every queued event (PRD 4.3).\npackage breaker\n\nimport (\n\t\"sync\"\n\t\"time\"\n)\n\ntype state struct {\n\tconsecutive int\n\t// openedAt + coolDown is the end of the current pause.\n\topenedAt time.Time\n\tcoolDown time.Duration\n}\n\ntype Breaker struct {\n\tthreshold   int\n\tcoolDown    time.Duration\n\tmaxCoolDown time.Duration\n\n\tmu     sync.Mutex\n\tstates map[string]*state\n}\n\nfunc New(threshold int, coolDown, maxCoolDown time.Duration) *Breaker {\n\treturn &Breaker{\n\t\tthreshold:   threshold,\n\t\tcoolDown:    coolDown,\n\t\tmaxCoolDown: maxCoolDown,\n\t\tstates:      make(map[string]*state),\n\t}\n}\n\n// Allow reports whether a request to endpoint may be attempted at now.\n// After an expired cool-down traffic is allowed again (a half-open probe): if\n// the endpoint is still broken the next failure re-opens the breaker with a\n// longer pause; if it recovered, RecordSuccess closes it for good.\nfunc (b *Breaker) Allow(endpoint string, now time.Time) bool {\n\tb.mu.Lock()\n\tdefer b.mu.Unlock()\n\tst := b.states[endpoint]\n\tif st == nil || st.coolDown == 0 {\n\t\treturn true\n\t}\n\treturn !now.Before(st.openedAt.Add(st.coolDown))\n}\n\n// RetryAt returns when Allow becomes true again. Events held by an open\n// breaker are rescheduled to this time so no worker waits on the endpoint.\nfunc (b *Breaker) RetryAt(endpoint string, now time.Time) time.Time {\n\tb.mu.Lock()\n\tdefer b.mu.Unlock()\n\tst := b.states[endpoint]\n\tif st == nil || st.coolDown == 0 {\n\t\treturn now\n\t}\n\treturn st.openedAt.Add(st.coolDown)\n}\n\n// RecordSuccess closes the breaker for endpoint.\nfunc (b *Breaker) RecordSuccess(endpoint string) {\n\tb.mu.Lock()\n\tdefer b.mu.Unlock()\n\tif st := b.states[endpoint]; st != nil {\n\t\tst.consecutive = 0\n\t\tst.coolDown = 0\n\t}\n}\n\n// RecordFailure counts a consecutive failure and opens the breaker once the\n// threshold is reached. Each further consecutive failure doubles the cool-down\n// (capped): a broken endpoint is probed less and less often. Returns true when\n// this failure opened (or re-opened) the breaker.\nfunc (b *Breaker) RecordFailure(endpoint string, now time.Time) bool {\n\tb.mu.Lock()\n\tdefer b.mu.Unlock()\n\n\tst := b.states[endpoint]\n\tif st == nil {\n\t\tst = &state{}\n\t\tb.states[endpoint] = st\n\t}\n\tst.consecutive++\n\tif st.consecutive < b.threshold {\n\t\treturn false\n\t}\n\n\t// consecutive == threshold -> 1x coolDown, threshold+1 -> 2x, ...\n\tshift := st.consecutive - b.threshold\n\tcd := b.coolDown\n\tfor i := 0; i < shift && cd < b.maxCoolDown; i++ {\n\t\tcd *= 2\n\t}\n\tif cd > b.maxCoolDown {\n\t\tcd = b.maxCoolDown\n\t}\n\tst.openedAt = now\n\tst.coolDown = cd\n\treturn true\n}\n\n// ConsecutiveFailures reports the current streak; used by tests and logs.\nfunc (b *Breaker) ConsecutiveFailures(endpoint string) int {\n\tb.mu.Lock()\n\tdefer b.mu.Unlock()\n\tif st := b.states[endpoint]; st != nil {\n\t\treturn st.consecutive\n\t}\n\treturn 0\n}\n",

Empty on success.\nfunc (r Result) AttemptError() string {\n\tif r.Err != nil {\n\t\treturn r.Err.Error()\n\t}\n\tif r.StatusCode < 200 || r.StatusCode > 299 {\n\t\ts := fmt.Sprintf(\"HTTP %d\", r.StatusCode)\n\t\tif r.BodySnippet != \"\" {\n\t\t\ts += \": \" + r.BodySnippet\n\t\t}\n\t\treturn s\n\t}\n\treturn \"\"\n}\n\ntype Sender struct {\n\tclient  *http.Client\n\ttimeout time.Duration\n\tnow     func() time.Time\n}\n\n// NewSender builds a sender with the per-request timeout. now is the injected\n// clock; nil means time.Now.\nfunc NewSender(timeout time.Duration, now func() time.Time) *Sender {\n\tif now == nil {\n\t\tnow = time.Now\n\t}\n\treturn &Sender{\n\t\tclient: &http.Client{\n\t\t\tTimeout: timeout,\n\t\t\t// Never follow redirects: a 3xx is an answer that must be\n\t\t\t// classified and recorded, and following would silently POST the\n\t\t\t// payload to a different host than the customer registered.\n\t\t\tCheckRedirect: func(*http.Request, []*http.Request) error {\n\t\t\t\treturn http.ErrUseLastResponse\n\t\t\t},\n\t\t},\n\t\ttimeout: timeout,\n\t\tnow:     now,\n\t}\n}\n\n// body is the exact payload customers receive (PRD section 7).\ntype body struct {\n\tEventID   string          `json:\"event_id\"`\n\tCreatedAt time.Time       `json:\"created_at\"`\n\tSequence  int64           `json:\"sequence\"`\n\tPayload   json.RawMessage `json:\"payload\"`\n}\n\n// Send POSTs the event to endpoint. attempt is the 1-based attempt number,\n// reported in the X-Delivery-Attempt header.\n//\n// The request runs on a context that is not cancelled by shutdown (so\n// graceful shutdown waits for in-flight requests) but is always bounded by the\n// per-request timeout, which is what releases a worker parked on a slow\n// endpoint.\nfunc (s *Sender) Send(ctx context.Context, endpoint string, ev store.Event, attempt int) Result {\n\tpayload, err := json.Marshal(body{\n\t\tEventID:   ev.ID,\n\t\tCreatedAt: ev.CreatedAt.UTC(),\n\t\tSequence:  ev.Sequence,\n\t\tPayload:   ev.Payload,\n\t})\n\tif err != nil {\n\t\t// Only possible with a broken payload; not a transport failure.\n\t\treturn Result{StartedAt: s.now(), Err: fmt.Errorf(\"encode delivery body: %w\", err)}\n\t}\n\n\tctx, cancel := context.WithTimeout(context.WithoutCancel(ctx), s.timeout)\n\tdefer cancel()\n\n\treq, err := http.NewRequestWithContext(ctx, http.MethodPost, endpoint, bytes.NewReader(payload))\n\tif err != nil {\n\t\treturn Result{StartedAt: s.now(), Err: fmt.Errorf(\"build request: %w\", err)}\n\t}\n\treq.Header.Set(\"X-Event-Id\", ev.ID)\n\treq.Header.Set(\"X-Delivery-Attempt\", strconv.Itoa(attempt))\n\treq.Header.Set(\"Content-Type\", \"application/json\")\n\n\tstarted := s.now()\n\tresp, err := s.client.Do(req)\n\tif err != nil {\n\t\treturn Result{\n\t\t\tStartedAt: started,\n\t\t\tDuration:  s.now().Sub(started),\n\t\t\tErr:       fmt.Errorf(\"post %s: %w\", endpoint, err),\n\t\t}\n\t}\n\tdefer resp.Body.Close()\n\n\tres := Result{\n\t\tStartedAt:  started,\n\t\tDuration:   s.now().Sub(started),\n\t\tStatusCode: resp.StatusCode,\n\t}\n\t// Bounded drain: enough for a useful error snippet, and it lets the\n\t// connection be reused instead of leaked.\n\tif snippet, _ := io.ReadAll(io.LimitReader(resp.Body, snippetLimit)); len(snippet) > 0 {\n\t\tres.BodySnippet = string(snippet)\n\t}\n\tif d, ok := retry.RetryAfter(resp.Header, s.now()); ok {\n\t\tres.RetryAfter, res.HasRetryAfter = d, true\n\t}\n\treturn res\n}\n",

{
  "content": "// Package dispatcher runs the fixed worker pool: claim a due event, deliver it,\n// record the attempt, then mark delivered / schedule the retry / fail it.\npackage dispatcher\n\nimport (\n\t\"context\"\n\t\"log/slog\"\n\t\"math/rand\"\n\t\"sync\"\n\t\"time\"\n\n\t\"webhook-delivery-service/internal/breaker\"\n\t\"webhook-delivery-service/internal/config\"\n\t\"webhook-delivery-service/internal/delivery\"\n\t\"webhook-delivery-service/internal/retry\"\n\t\"webhook-delivery-service/internal/store\"\n)\n\ntype Dispatcher struct {\n\tcfg     config.Config\n\tstore   *store.Store\n\tsender  *delivery.Sender\n\tbreaker *breaker.Breaker\n\tlog     *slog.Logger\n\tnow     func() time.Time\n\trnd     func() float64\n}\n\n// New wires the dispatcher. log, now and rnd may be nil (sensible defaults),\n// which keeps tests small.\nfunc New(\n\tcfg config.Config,\n\tst *store.Store,\n\tsender *delivery.Sender,\n\tbrk *breaker.Breaker,\n\tlog *slog.Logger,\n\tnow func() time.Time,\n\trnd func() float64,\n) *Dispatcher {\n\tif log == nil {\n\t\tlog = slog.Default()\n\t}\n\tif now == nil {\n\t\tnow = time.Now\n\t}\n\tif rnd == nil {\n\t\trnd = rand.Float64\n\t}\n\treturn &Dispatcher{cfg: cfg, store: st, sender: sender, breaker: brk, log: log, now: now, rnd: rnd}\n}\n\n// Run starts the worker pool and blocks until ctx is cancelled and every\n// worker has finished its current event (graceful shutdown: stop claiming,\n// let in-flight work finish).\nfunc (d *Dispatcher) Run(ctx context.Context) {\n\td.log.Info(\"dispatcher started\",\n\t\t\"workers\", d.cfg.Workers, \"per_customer_cap\", d.cfg.PerCustomerCap,\n\t\t\"poll_interval\", d.cfg.PollInterval, \"request_timeout\", d.cfg.RequestTimeout)\n\n\tvar wg sync.WaitGroup\n\tfor i := 0; i < d.cfg.Workers; i++ {\n\t\twg.Add(1)\n\t\tgo func(id int) {\n\t\t\tdefer wg.Done()\n\t\t\td.worker(ctx, id)\n\t\t}(i)\n\t}\n\twg.Wait()\n\td.log.Info(\"dispatcher stopped\")\n}\n\nfunc (d *Dispatcher) worker(ctx context.Context, id int) {\n\tfor {\n\t\t// Checked before every claim so shutdown stops taking new work.\n\t\tif ctx.Err() != nil {\n\t\t\treturn\n\t\t}\n\t\tev, ok, err := d.store.ClaimDue(ctx, d.now(), d.now().Add(d.cfg.LeaseDuration), d.cfg.PerCustomerCap)\n\t\tif err != nil {\n\t\t\tif ctx.Err() != nil {\n\t\t\t\treturn\n\t\t\t}\n\t\t\td.log.Error(\"claim event\", \"worker\", id, \"error\", err)\n\t\t}\n\t\tif !ok {\n\t\t\tif !sleep(ctx, d.cfg.PollInterval) {\n\t\t\t\treturn\n\t\t\t}\n\t\t\tcontinue\n\t\t}\n\t\t// WithoutCancel: shutdown must not abort the bookkeeping of work that\n\t\t// has already been claimed; the request timeout bounds the network part.\n\t\td.process(context.WithoutCancel(ctx), id, ev)\n\t}\n}\n\n// process runs one delivery attempt for a claimed event and applies the\n// resulting state transition.\nfunc (d *Dispatcher) process(ctx context.Context, workerID int, ev store.Event) {\n\tlog := d.log.With(\n\t\t\"event_id\", ev.ID, \"subscription_id\", ev.SubscriptionID,\n\t\t\"customer_id\", ev.CustomerID, \"worker\", workerID,\n\t)\n\tnow := d.now()\n\n\t// Terminal deadline (FR6). An event claimed at or after its deadline is\n\t// failed without another attempt, which is what bounds the retry storm.\n\tdeadline := ev.CreatedAt.Add(d.cfg.Retry.Deadline)\n\tif !now.Before(deadline) {\n\t\treason := \"retry deadline exceeded\"\n\t\tif ev.LastError != \"\" {\n\t\t\treason += \": \" + ev.LastError\n\t\t}\n\t\tif err := d.store.MarkFailed(ctx, ev.ID, reason, now); err != nil {\n\t\t\tlog.Error(\"mark failed\", \"error\", err)\n\t\t\treturn\n\t\t}\n\t\tlog.Warn(\"event failed\", \"reason\", reason, \"attempts\", ev.AttemptCount)\n\t\treturn\n\t}\n\n\t// Open breaker: do not burn an attempt, push the event to the moment the\n\t// breaker expects to be closed again and go claim something else. This is\n\t// what keeps a dead endpoint from parking workers or eating its backlog.\n\tif !d.breaker.Allow(ev.EndpointURL, now) {\n\t\tretryAt := d.breaker.RetryAt(ev.EndpointURL, now)\n\t\tif err := d.store.Reschedule(ctx, ev.ID, retryAt, \"circuit breaker open\"); err != nil {\n\t\t\tlog.Error(\"reschedule for breaker\", \"error\", err)\n\t\t}\n\t\tlog.Debug(\"held by circuit breaker\", \"endpoint\", ev.EndpointURL, \"retry_at\", retryAt)\n\t\treturn\n\t}\n\n\tattempt := ev.AttemptCount + 1\n\tres := d.sender.Send(ctx, ev.EndpointURL, ev, attempt)\n\n\t// Every attempt leaves a row, whatever the outcome (status, error, duration).\n\tattemptErr := res.AttemptError()\n\tif err := d.store.RecordAttempt(ctx, ev.ID, store.Attempt{\n\t\tAttempt:     attempt,\n\t\tAttemptedAt: res.StartedAt,\n\t\tHTTPStatus:  statusCode(res),\n\t\tError:       attemptErr,\n\t\tDurationMS:  res.Duration.Milliseconds(),\n\t}); ```go
func main() {
	log := slog.New(slog.NewTextHandler(os.Stdout, &slog.HandlerOptions{Level: slog.LevelInfo}))
	cfg, err := config.Load()
	if err != nil { log.Error("config", "error", err); os.Exit(1) }
	if err := cfg.Validate(); err != nil ... (Load already validates)
	
	st, err := store.Open(cfg.DBPath, nil)
	defer closeseed subscriptions if cfg.SeedFile != ""
	
	sender := delivery.NewSender(cfg.RequestTimeout, nil)
	brk := breaker.New(cfg.Breaker.Threshold, cfg.Breaker.CoolDown, cfg.Breaker.MaxCoolDown)
	disp := dispatcher.New(cfg, st, sender, brk, log, nil, nil)
	apiSrv := api.New(st, cfg, log)
	
	httpSrv := &http.Server{Addr: cfg.Addr, Handler: apiSrv.Routes(), ReadHeaderTimeout: 5s}
	
	rootCtx, stop := signal.NotifyContext(context.Background(), os.Interrupt, syscall.SIGTERM)
	defer stop()
	
	dctx, dcancel := context.WithCancel(context.Background())
	workersDone := make(chan struct{})
	go func(){ disp.Run(dctx); close(workersDone) }()
	
	serverErr := make(chan error, 1)
	go func(){ log.Info("listening", "addr", cfg.Addr); serverErr <- httpSrv.ListenAndServe() }()
	
	select {
	case err := <-serverErr:
		if err != nil && !errors.Is(err, http.ErrServerClosed) { log.Error("http server", "error", err); dcancel(); <-workersDone; os.Exit(1) }
	case <-rootCtx.Done(): log.Info("shutdown signal received")
	}
	
	// Stop claiming; in-flight deliveries finish (bounded by request timeout).
	dcancel()
	shutdownCtx, cancel := context.WithTimeout(context.Background(), cfg.ShutdownTimeout)
	defer cancel()
	if err := httpSrv.Shutdown(shutdownCtx); err != nil { log.Error("http shutdown", "error", err) }
	select {
	case <-workersDone:
		log.Info("shutdown complete")
	case <-shutdownCtx.Done():
		log.Warn("shutdown timeout waiting for in-flight deliveries; exiting (leases will be retried after restart)")
	}
	_ = st.Close()
```

func newEnv(t *testing.T, cfg config.Config) *env {
	t.Helper()
	st, err := store.Open(filepath.Join(t.TempDir(), "test.db"), nil)
	...
	brk := breaker.New(...)
	sender := delivery.NewSender(cfg.RequestTimeout, nil)
	disp := dispatcher.New(cfg, st, sender, brk, slog.New(slog.NewTextHandler(io.Discard, nil)), nil, nil)
	ctx, cancel := context.WithCancel(context.Background())
	done := make(chan struct{})
	go func(){ disp.Run(ctx); close(done) }()
	t.Cleanup(func(){ cancel(); select{ case <-done: case <-time.After(5*time.Second): t.Error("dispatcher did not stop") }; st.Close() })
	return &env{...}
}

Dispatcher scenario tests: internal/dispatcher/scenario_test.go

```go
package dispatcher

import (...)

type harness struct {
	t *testing.T
	cfg config.Config
	st *store.Store
	brk *breaker.Breaker
	cancel context.CancelFunc
	done chan struct{}
}

func newHarness(t *testing.T, tune func(*config.Config)) *harness {
	cfg := testConfig()
	if tune != nil { tune(&cfg) }
	if err := cfg.Validate(); err != nil { t.Fatalf("config: %v", err) }
	st, err := store.Open(filepath.Join(t.TempDir(), "dispatch.db"), nil)
	...
	brk := breaker.New(cfg.Breaker.Threshold, cfg.Breaker.CoolDown, cfg.Breaker.MaxCoolDown)
	d := New(cfg, st, delivery.NewSender(cfg.RequestTimeout, nil), brk, slog.New(slog.NewTextHandler(io.Discard, nil)), nil, nil)
	ctx, cancel := context.WithCancel(context.Background())
	done := make(chan struct{})
	go func() { d.Run(ctx); close(done) }()
	t.Cleanup(func() {
		cancel()
		select {
		case <-done:
		case <-time.After(5 * time.Second):
			t.Error("dispatcher did not shut down in time")
		}
		if err := st.Close(); err != nil { t.Errorf(...) }
	})
	return &harness{...}
}
```

```go
func waitFor(t *testing.T, timeout time.Duration, what string, cond func() bool) {
	t.Helper()
	deadline := time.Now().Add(timeout)
	for time.Now().Before(deadline) {
		if cond() { return }
		time.Sleep(5 * time.Millisecond)
	}
	t.Fatalf("timed out after %s waiting for %s", timeout, what)
}
```

endpoint.\n\twaitFor(3*time.Second, \"circuit breaker to open\", func() bool {\n\t\treturn !h.breaker.Allow(badSrv.URL, time.Now())\n\t})\n\tif h.breaker.ConsecutiveFailures(badSrv.URL) < h.cfg.Breaker.Threshold {\n\t\tt.Errorf(\"consecutive failures = %d, want >= %d\",\n\t\t\th.breaker.ConsecutiveFailures(badSrv.URL), h.cfg.Breaker.Threshold)\n\t}\n\n\t// Past the deadline the stuck events become terminal `failed` and stay.\n\twaitFor(5*time.Second, \"stuck events to fail after the deadline\", func() bool {\n\t\treturn h.event(stuckA.ID).Status == store.StatusFailed &&\n\t\t\th.event(stuckB.ID).Status == store.StatusFailed\n\t})\n\tfor _, id := range []string{stuckA.ID, stuckB.ID} {\n\t\tgot := h.event(id)\n\t\tif !strings.Contains(got.LastError, \"retry deadline want 2 failed (acme) and 2 delivered (globex)\", counts)\n\t}\n\tif badMock.Count() == 0 || goodMock.Count() == 0 {\n\t\tt.Errorf(\"both endpoints should have been called: bad=%d good=%d\", false\n\t\t\t}\n\t\t}\n\t\treturn true\n\t})\n\n\tif got := mock.MaxInFlight(); got > h.cfg.PerCustomerCap {\n\t\tt.Errorf(\"peak concurrent requests = %d, want <= per-customer cap %d\", got, strings.Contains(ev.LastError, \"deadline exceeded\") {\n\t\t\tsawTimeout = true\n\t\t}\n\t\tif len(ev.Attempts) == 0 {\n\t\t\tt.Errorf(\"event %s has no attempt history\", id)\n\t\t}\n\t}\n\tif Draft:

```markdown
# DECISIONS.md

Run: `go run ./cmd/server` (SQLite file `webhook.db`, port `:8080`; every value overridable by env vars, see `internal/config`).
Mock customer endpoint: `go run ./cmd/mockendpoint -mode ok|fail|slow|flaky|down`.

## Delivery semantics — at-least-once
The event is written to SQLite before the 202 ... duplicates possible ... event_id idempotency key ... why not at-most-once / exactly-once.

## Retry
Table/short: 10s initial, 2x, ±20% jitter, 30min cap, 24h deadline, 10s per-request timeout (all configurable). Retry on network errors/timeouts/5xx/408/429 (Retry-After honoured); other 4xx fail early with the reason recorded. Terminal `failed` rows and attempt history are never deleted. Jitter is applied before the max cap, so a delay never exceeds 30min.

## Long outages
Shared pool of 8 workers, oldest-due-first, no worker parked on a dead endpoint (failed delivery = row with future next_attempt_at + lease-free). Per-customer cap 2 in-flight leases enforced inside the claim SQL. Breaker per endpoint after 5 consecutive failures with doubling cool-down (30s → 10min cap); held events are rescheduled to the breaker's retry time without burning an attempt. Trade-off: less isolation than per-customer queues; a backlog's first attempts can be deferred while the breaker is open.

## Ordering
No strict guarantee ... per-customer sequence, event_id, created_at in the payload ... customers dedupe/reorder. Strict ordering would let one bad event block the queue.

## Crash safety
Claim sets lease_until in the same transaction as the claim (single-writer SQLite connection); expired lease = claimable again → at-least-once across restarts.

## Deliberately left out (next steps)
- Auth, multi-tenant billing, deployment/CI, UI (out of scope).
- Multi-instance operation: single process, single SQLite connection; would need Postgres + SKIP LOCKED.
- Per-customer queues / strict ordering mode / replay API: failed events are retained and can be replayed manually later.
- Dedupe at ingest: event_id is generated per accepted POST; producers that retry after a lost response get two events (customers dedupe by payload/event_id? hmm)

Wait — careful with honesty: ingest dedupe. PRD's FR1 mentions event_id as idempotency key for customers. If a producer re-POSTs after losing the response, we create a second event. Note it.

Also mention: `attempt` column added to delivery_attempts (small deviation from PRD schema) — flag it.

Keep to ~half page-ish; it's fine to be a bit more since it must cover 5 topics.
```

**Tool: write**

**Input:**
```json
{
  "content": "# DECISIONS.md\n\nWebhook delivery service — Go, single process.\n\n- Run: `go run ./cmd/server` (SQLite file `webhook.db`, listens on `:8080`). Every\n  time-based parameter is an env var with the PRD default; see `internal/config`.\n- Mock customer endpoint: `go run ./cmd/mockendpoint -mode ok|fail|slow|flaky|down`.\n- Seed subscriptions: `SEED_FILE=seed.json` with\n  `[{\"customer_id\":\"acme\",\"endpoint_url\":\"http://localhost:9090/hook\"}]`, or\n  `POST /subscriptions`.\n\n## Delivery semantics: at-least-once\n\nThe event is committed to SQLite **before** the 202 is written; delivery is\nasynchronous. A timeout cannot be distinguished from a slow success, so it is\nretried and **duplicates are possible** — `event_id` is the idempotency key and\nhandlers must be idempotent. At-most-once would silently lose events (against\nthe core requirement); exactly-once is not achievable over HTTP without the\nreceiver's cooperation. A consequence we accepted: if a producer re-posts after\nlosing the response, it gets a second event — we do not dedupe at ingest.\n\n## Retry\n\nExponential backoff with jitter: initial 10s, ×2, ±20% jitter, 30min cap,\n24h deadline from creation, 10s per-request timeout (all configurable).\nRetry on network errors, timeouts, 5xx, 408 and 429 (honouring `Retry-After`);\nany other 4xx fails immediately with the reason recorded. `failed` rows and the\nfull `delivery_attempts` history are never deleted, so events stay replayable.\nJitter is applied *before* the cap, so a delay never exceeds 30min; a\n`MIN_RETRY_GAP` floor keeps an aggressive `Retry-After` from becoming a hot loop.\n\n## Long outages\n\nA shared pool of 8 workers claims due rows oldest-first; a failed delivery is\nonly a row with a future `next_attempt_at`, so **no worker is ever parked on a\ndead endpoint** and a six-hour outage costs nothing between attempts. The claim\nquery enforces the per-customer cap (2 in-flight leases) in SQL, so one slow\ncustomer cannot occupy the pool. After 5 consecutive failures an endpoint's\nbreaker opens with a doubling cool-down (30s → 10min cap); queued events for that\nendpoint are rescheduled to the breaker's retry time **without burning an\nattempt**. Trade-off: less isolation than dedicated per-customer queues, and\nwhile the breaker is open a backlog's first attempts are deferred.\n\n## Ordering: no strict guarantee\n\nOldest-due-first gives roughly chronological delivery in the normal case, but\nretries and concurrency reorder events. Every delivery therefore carries\n`event_id`, `created_at` and a per-customer `sequence` so consumers can dedupe\nand reorder. Strict ordering would need per-customer serialization, letting one\nfailing event block everything behind it — the exact failure mode we are\navoiding.\n\n## Crash safety\n\nA claim sets `lease_until` in the same transaction as the claim (single-writer\nSQLite connection ⇒ atomic). An expired lease simply becomes claimable again, so\na crash mid-delivery is retried after restart — consistent with at-least-once.\n\n## Deliberately left out (next steps)\n\n- Auth, multi-tenant billing, deployment/CI and any UI — out of scope per the brief.\n- Multi-instance operation: single process, single SQLite connection. The next\n  step is Postgres with `FOR UPDATE SKIP LOCKED`.\n- Per-customer queues, an opt-in ordered mode, and a replay API: `failed` events\n  are retained so a replay endpoint is a small addition later.\n- Not a deviation but worth flagging: `delivery_attempts` has an extra `attempt`\n  column (attempt number) beyond the PRD schema, and `GET /subscriptions` exists\n  for inspection.\n",
  "path": "/Users/keenanadityo/Documents/programming/webhook-delivery-service/DECISIONS.md"
}
```

## Assistant

_Thinking:_

Everything is clean and complete. Now write the final report covering each of the 6 steps: what changed, why, trade-offs; deviations; what's left; verification results.

Keep it organized but reasonably concise.

The project is built, tested and verified end-to-end. Summary by step:

## What was built

**Step 1 — setup, schema, subscriptions**
- `go.mod` (module `webhook-delivery-service`), `internal/config` (every PRD default, env-overridable, `Validate()` refuses inconsistent values e.g. lease ≤ request timeout), `internal/store` (SQLite + migrations, `subscriptions` / `events` / `delivery_attempts`, index on `(status, next_attempt_at)`).
- **Claim** (`internal/store/claim.go`) is one SELECT + lease UPDATE in a single transaction, oldest-due-first, with the per-customer cap counted in SQL. *Why:* two workers can never take the same row; the cap lives where the lease does, so it can't drift. *Trade-off:* the DB uses **one connection** (`SetMaxOpenConns(1)`) — that's what makes the transaction atomic with zero busy-wait code in a single-process service; it would be the first thing to change for multi-instance.
- Timestamps are fixed-width strings (not RFC3339Nano) so lexicographic ordering in SQL is correct.

**Step 2 — ingest + status API** (`internal/api`)
- `POST /events` → 202 after commit; `GET /events/{id}` (with attempt history), `GET /events?subscription_id=&customer_id=&status=&limit=`, `POST`/`GET /subscriptions`, `GET /healthz` (pending/delivered/failed counters read live from SQLite).
- Validation strictly before storing: unknown subscription → 404, malformed JSON → 400, oversized payload → 413; a test asserts **zero rows** after every rejected request (FR2).

**Step 3 — dispatcher, workers, retry**
- `internal/retry`: `Delay` (initial ×2^n, ±20% jitter, 30 min cap, overflow-safe) and `Classify` (retry on transport error/5xx/408/429 incl. `Retry-After`; other 4xx fail early; 3xx retry, redirects never followed), `internal/delivery` (sender with required headers + `{event_id, created_at, sequence, payload}` body, bounded response-body snippet for diagnostics).
- `internal/dispatcher`: fixed pool (8), claim → deadline check → breaker check → send → **always** record the attempt → delivered/retry/failed. Retries are scheduled via `next_attempt_at`, so no worker ever waits on a dead endpoint.

**Step 4 — per-customer cap + circuit breaker**
- Cap enforced inside the claim query; `internal/breaker` opens after 5 consecutive failures with a doubling cool-down (30 s → 10 min cap), and held events are rescheduled to the breaker's retry time **without burning an attempt**.

**Step 5 — mock endpoint + tests**
- `internal/mockendpoint` (modes `ok|fail|slow|flaky|down`, tracks peak concurrency and every received request) shared by `cmd/mockendpoint` and the tests.
- Unit tests: backoff (bounds, jitter range, cap, overflow) and classification. Scenario tests: happy path (exactly one delivery + wire-format assertions), down-then-recovers (4 attempts, 500/500/500/200, growing gaps), long outage isolation (globex delivered in <500 ms while acme fails, breaker opens, acme ends `failed` at the deadline), slow endpoint (peak concurrency ≤ cap, all events end `failed` with a recorded timeout), crash mid-delivery (lease respected, redelivered after expiry, duplicate delivered with the same `event_id`).
- Store-level tests for claim ordering, lease expiry, cap and sequence.

**Step 6 — `DECISIONS.md`** covering semantics, retry, outages, ordering, crash safety and what was left out.

## Verification (actually run)
- `gofmt -l` clean, `go vet ./...` clean, `go build ./...` clean.
- `go test ./...` and `go test -race ./...` pass; dispatcher/API repeated `-count=3` without flakes (~5 s for the whole scenario suite).
- Manual end-to-end: `go run ./cmd/server` + mock endpoint → 404/400 rejections, 202, retries at ~300 ms then ~620 ms, `delivered` on attempt 3, `/healthz` counters, `SEED_FILE` loading, and graceful shutdown logged (`shutdown signal received` → `dispatcher stopped` → `shutdown complete`).

## Deviations / flags for you
1. **Layout:** added `internal/mockendpoint` (shared fake endpoint for `cmd/mockendpoint` and tests) — suggested layout didn't have a home for it.
2. **Schema:** `delivery_attempts` has an extra `attempt` column (attempt number) beyond the PRD's listed columns.
3. **API additions:** `GET /subscriptions` (inspection) and `MIN_RETRY_GAP` config (a `Retry-After: 0` floor so an aggressive header can't hot-loop). Both are noted in `DECISIONS.md`.
4. **Behaviour worth knowing:** while a breaker is open, a backlog's first attempts are deferred (by design — no attempts burned), which the slow-endpoint test surfaced.

## Not done / left
- No ingest-level dedupe (re-POSTing after a lost response creates a second event) — noted as a next step.
- `cmd/*` has no tests (they're thin entrypoints; behavior is covered in `internal/...`).
- No git commands were run; all files are untracked and yours to commit.
